\chapter{Exposé VIII. The finiteness
theorem}\label{exposuxe9-viii.-the-finiteness-theorem}

\section[1. A biduality spectral sequence]{\texorpdfstring{1. A
biduality spectral
sequence\footnote{The reader familiar with the language of Verdier's
  derived categories will recognize the spectral sequence associated
  with a biduality isomorphism. Cf. SGA 6 I.}}{1. A biduality spectral sequence}}\label{a-biduality-spectral-sequenceviii-1-1}

Let us state the result we want to reach:

\textbf{Proposition.}

Let \(A\) be a noetherian ring and let \(I\) be an ideal of \(A\). Set
\(X = \operatorname{Spec}(A)\) and \(Y = V(I)\). Let \(M\) be a finitely
generated \(A\)-module of finite projective dimension. Let
\(F = \tilde{M}\) be the \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module associated with
\(M\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  There exists a spectral sequence
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E₂\^{}\{p,q\} = Ext\^{}p\_Y(Ext\^{}\{{-}q\}(M, A), A) ⇒ H\^{}\{p+q\}\_Y(X, F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  There exists a spectral sequence
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E₂\^{}\{p,q\} = Ext\^{}p\_Y(Ext\^{}\{{-}q\}(F, 𝒪\_X), 𝒪\_X) ⇒ H\^{}\{p+q\}\_Y(X, F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Of course, 2) is deduced from 1) by remarking that, if \(M\) and \(N\)
are two finitely generated \(A\)-modules, and if one sets
\(F = \tilde{M}\) and \(G = \tilde{N}\), then one has isomorphisms

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ℋ\^{}•\_Y(F) ≅ H̃\^{}•\_Y(X, F),}
\NormalTok{Ext\^{}•\_Y(F, G) ≅ Ẽxt\^{}•\_Y(F, G),}
\NormalTok{Ext\^{}•\_\{𝒪\_X\}(F, G) ≅ Ẽxt\^{}•\_A(M, N).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(C\) be the category of \(A\)-modules and \texttt{Ab} that of
abelian groups. Let \(F\) be the functor

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{F : C → Ab    defined by    M ↦ Γ\_Y(M̃).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We know from Exposé II that there is an isomorphism of
\(\partial\)-functors

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}•\_Y(X, M̃) ≅ R\^{}• F(M).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Furthermore, let \(Ext^{\bullet}_{Y}\) denote the right derived functors
in the second argument of

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{F ∘ Hom : C° × C → Ab.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We know from Exposé VI that there is an isomorphism of
\(\partial\)-functors

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Ext\^{}•\_Y(M, N) ≃ Ext\^{}•\_Y(M̃, Ñ).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let us finally record the following result from Exposé VI: if \(C\) is
an injective \(A\)-module and \(N\) is a finitely generated
\(A\)-module, then the sheaf
\(\operatorname{Hom}(\tilde{N}, \tilde{C}) \cong \operatorname{Hom}\tilde{N, C}\)
is flasque, hence

\[ R^{1} F(\operatorname{Hom}(N, C)) = 0. \]

It remains to prove the following result:

\textbf{Lemma.}

Let \(A\) be a noetherian ring and let \(C\) be the category of
\(A\)-modules. Let \(F : C \to Ab\) be a left exact additive functor
such that, for every finitely generated \(A\)-module \(N\) and every
injective \(A\)-module \(C\), one has \(R^{1}
F(\operatorname{Hom}(N, C)) = 0\). Let \(M\) be a finitely generated
\(A\)-module of finite projective dimension. Then there exists a
spectral sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E₂\^{}\{p,q\} = Ext\^{}p\_F(Ext\^{}\{{-}q\}(M, A), A) ⇒ R\^{}\{p+q\} F(M),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(Ext^{p}_{F}\) denotes the \(p\)-th right derived functor of
\(F \circ \operatorname{Hom}\).

We shall consider only complexes whose differential has degree
\texttt{+1}. By the hypothesis on \(M\), there exists a projective
resolution of \(M\) of finite length

\[ u : L^{\bullet} \to M, \]

where, moreover, the \(L^{p}\) are finitely generated modules and
\(L^{p} = 0\) if \(p \notin [-n, 0]\). Let, on the other hand,
\(v : M \to I^{\bullet}\) be an injective resolution of \(M\). We claim
that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{v ∘ u : L\^{}• → I\^{}•}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is an injective resolution of \(L^{\bullet}\). We must specify what this
means.

\textbf{Definition.}

Let \(X^{\bullet}\) be a complex of \(A\)-modules; by an \emph{injective
resolution} of \(X^{\bullet}\) one means a homomorphism of complexes

\[ x : X^{\bullet} \to C^{\bullet}_{X}, \]

such that \(C^{p}_{X}\) is injective for every \(p \in \mathbb{Z}\), and
such that \(x\) induces an isomorphism on homology.

\textbf{Proposition.}

Every left-bounded complex --- i.e.~such that there exists
\(q \in \mathbb{Z}\) with \(X^{p} = 0\) for \(p < q\) --- admits an
injective resolution. Moreover, if \(u : X^{\bullet} \to Y^{\bullet}\)
is a homomorphism of complexes (both left-bounded) and if
\(x : X^{\bullet} \to C^{\bullet}_{X}\) and
\(y : Y^{\bullet} \to C^{\bullet}_{Y}\) are injective resolutions of
\(X^{\bullet}\) and \(Y^{\bullet}\), then there exists a homomorphism of
complexes

\[ C_{u} : C^{\bullet}_{X} \to C^{\bullet}_{Y}, \]

unique up to homotopy, such that the diagram

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{        x}
\NormalTok{X\^{}•  ────────►  C\_X\^{}•}
\NormalTok{ │                │}
\NormalTok{ u                C\_u}
\NormalTok{ │                │}
\NormalTok{ ▼      y         ▼}
\NormalTok{Y\^{}•  ────────►  C\_Y\^{}•}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is commutative up to homotopy.

The proof is left to the reader.\footnote{Cf. also H. Cartan and S.
  Eilenberg, \emph{Homological Algebra}, Princeton Math. Series,
  vol.~19, Princeton University Press, 1956.}

Let us recall a notation introduced in Exposé V.

\textbf{Notation.}

Let \(X^{\bullet}\) and \(Y^{\bullet}\) be two complexes. We denote by
\(\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(X^{\bullet},
Y^{\bullet})\) the simple complex whose component of degree \(n\) is

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(Hom\^{}•(X\^{}•, Y\^{}•))\^{}n = ∏\_\{−p+q=n\} Hom(X\^{}p, Y\^{}q),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

also written \(\operatorname{Hom}^{n}(X^{\bullet}, Y^{\bullet})\), and
whose differential is given by

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{∂\^{}n : Hom\^{}n(X\^{}•, Y\^{}•) → Hom\^{}\{n+1\}(X\^{}•, Y\^{}•),}
\NormalTok{∂\^{}n = d′ + (−1)\^{}\{n+1\} d″,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(d'\) and \(d''\) are the differentials (of degree \texttt{+1})
induced by those of \(X^{\bullet}\) and \(Y^{\bullet}\).

Let then \(A^{\bullet}\) be the complex defined by \(A^{p} = 0\) if
\(p \neq 0\) and \(A^{0} = A\). Let

\[ a : A^{\bullet} \to C^{\bullet}_{A} \]

be an injective resolution of \(A^{\bullet}\). Consider the double
complex

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Q\^{}\{p,q\} = Hom(Hom(L\^{}\{{-}q\}, A), C\_A\^{}p).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The first spectral sequence of the bicomplex \(F Q^{\bullet\bullet}\)
will yield the conclusion of Lemma 1.2.

Set

\[ L'^{\bullet} = \operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(L^{\bullet}, A^{\bullet}), \]

and

\[ P^{\bullet} = \operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(L'^{\bullet}, C^{\bullet}_{A}). \]

One sees easily that \(P^{\bullet}\) is the simple complex associated
with \(Q^{\bullet\bullet}\). Let us compute the abutment of the spectral
sequence, i.e.~the homology of \(F P^{\bullet}\). For this, using the
fact that \(L^{\bullet}\) is finitely generated projective in every
dimension, one proves that \(L^{\bullet}\) is isomorphic to
\(\operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(L'^{\bullet}, A^{\bullet})\). From the
homomorphism \(a : A^{\bullet} \to C^{\bullet}_{A}\) one deduces a
homomorphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{b : Hom\^{}•(L′\^{}•, A\^{}•) → Hom\^{}•(L′\^{}•, C\_A\^{}•),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

or equivalently, a homomorphism

\[ c : L^{\bullet} \to P^{\bullet}. \]

This being said, it is easy to see, using the fact that \(L'^{\bullet}\)
is finitely generated projective in every dimension and left-bounded,
that (1.5) is an injective resolution of \(L^{\bullet}\). Applying
Proposition 1.4, one concludes that \(P^{\bullet}\) is
homotopy-equivalent to \(I^{\bullet}\), where \(I^{\bullet}\) is the
injective resolution of \(M\) introduced earlier (1.1). One deduces that
the abutment of the first spectral sequence of the double complex \(F
Q^{\bullet\bullet}\), which is \(H^{\bullet}(F P^{\bullet})\), is
isomorphic to \(R^{\bullet} F(M)\).

The initial term of the first spectral sequence of the bicomplex
\(F Q^{\bullet\bullet}\) is

\[ E^{p,q}_{2} = 'H^{p}(''H^{q}(F Q^{\bullet\bullet})). \]

For every \(p \in \mathbb{Z}\), \(C^{p}_{A}\) is injective. By the
hypothesis on \(F\), the functor (restricted to the category of finitely
generated modules)

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{N ↦ F Hom(N, C\_A\^{}p)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is exact. Hence one deduces isomorphisms

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{″H\^{}q(F Hom(L′\^{}•, C\_A\^{}p)) ≅ F Hom(H\^{}\{{-}q\}(L′\^{}•), C\_A\^{}p).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

By the definition of \(Ext^{\bullet}_{F}\) as the derived functor of
\(F \circ \operatorname{Hom}\), one deduces isomorphisms

\[ E^{p,q}_{2} \cong Ext^{p}_{F}(H^{-q}(L'^{\bullet}), A). \]

Now
\(L'^{\bullet} = \operatorname{Hom}^{\bullet}(L^{\bullet}, A^{\bullet})\),
where \(L^{\bullet}\) is a projective resolution of \(M\), whence
isomorphisms

\[ Ext^{-q}(M, A) \cong H^{-q}(L'^{\bullet}), \]

which gives the conclusion. QED.

\section{2. The finiteness theorem}\label{the-finiteness-theorem}

\textbf{Theorem.}\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} For an analogous statement, but
  in a somewhat more general situation, see Mme Raynaud (Raynaud M.,
  ``Théorèmes de Lefschetz en cohomologie des faisceaux cohérents et en
  cohomologie étale. Application au groupe fondamental'', \emph{Ann.
  Sci. Éc. Norm. Sup.} (4) \textbf{7} (1974), pp.~29--52, proposition
  II.2.1).}

Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian prescheme, \(Y\) a closed subset of
\(X\), and \(F\) a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module. Suppose that
\(X\) is locally embeddable in a regular prescheme.\footnote{This
  condition can be generalized to the hypothesis of the existence
  locally on \(X\) of a ``dualizing complex'', in the sense defined in
  R. Hartshorne, \emph{Residues and duality} (cited in footnote (*) of
  Exp. IV p.~46).} Let \(i \in \mathbb{Z}\). Suppose that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\tightlist
\item
  for every \(x \in U = X - Y\), one has
\end{enumerate}

\[ H^{i-c(x)}(F_{x}) = 0, \]

where one has set\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} As in Exposé V,
  \(H^{\bullet}_{x}(F)\) denotes the local cohomology
  \(H^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{m}_{x}}(F_{x})\).}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{c(x) = codim(\{x\}̄ ∩ Y, \{x\}̄).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Then:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  \(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{Y}(F)\) is coherent.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Corollary.}\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} Strictly speaking, this is a
  corollary of the proof that follows and not of the statement. The
  implication c) ⇒ a) is tautological. The other direction is not, but
  follows from the proof. To be precise: as below, one covers \(X\) by
  opens embeddable in regular schemes, which allows one, as explained
  below, to reduce to \(X
  = \operatorname{Spec}(A)\) affine regular and \(F = \tilde{M}\) where
  \(M\) is an \(A\)-module of finite projective dimension. It is shown
  in this case that conditions a) and c) are equivalent to the dual
  conditions a′) and c′). One then shows that c′) implies condition d)
  (see below) which itself implies a′). See the considerations following
  2.4.}

Under the hypotheses of the preceding theorem, condition a) is
equivalent to:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  for every \(x \in U\) such that \(c(x) = 1\), one has
  \(H^{i-1}(F_{x}) = 0\).
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Corollary.}

Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian prescheme that is locally embeddable
in a regular prescheme, let \(Y\) be a closed subset of \(X\), let \(F\)
be a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module, and let \(n\) be an integer.
The following conditions are equivalent:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  for every \(x \in U\), one has \(prof F_{x} > n - c(x)\);
\item
  for every \(x \in U\) such that \(c(x) = 1\), one has
  \(prof F_{x} \geqslant n\);
\item
  for every \(i \in \mathbb{Z}\), \(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{Y}(F)\) is coherent
  if \(i \leqslant n\);
\item
  \(R^{i} i_{*}(F|U)\) is coherent for \(i < n\).\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.}
    This condition appeared only in the body of the proof, but not in
    the statement of the corollary; since it is used in §3, we have
    added it.}
\end{enumerate}

Suppose these results acquired when \(X\) is the spectrum of a regular
noetherian ring \(A\) and when \(F\) is the sheaf associated with an
\(A\)-module of finite projective dimension.

Let us first remark that, if \((X_{j})_{j \in J}\) is an open covering
of \(X\) by opens embeddable in a regular scheme, then each of the above
conditions is equivalent to the conjunction of the analogous conditions
obtained by replacing \(X\) by \(X_{j}\), \(Y\) by
\(Y_{j} = Y \cap X_{j}\), and \(F\) by \(F|X_{j}\). Indeed, only the
conditions involving \(c(x)\) can present a difficulty. Let \(x \in U\).
If \(x \in X_{j}\), setting

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{c\_j(x) = codim(X\_j ∩ \{x\}̄ ∩ Y, X\_j ∩ \{x\}̄),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

one has necessarily \(c_{j}(x) \geqslant c(x)\). Let
\(y \in \bar{x} \cap Y\) which ``gives the codimension'', i.e.~such that
\(c(x) = \dim \mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}, y}\), and let \(X_{j}\) be an open
of the covering such that \(y \in X_{j}\); then \(x \in
X_{j}\), hence \(c_{j}(x) = c(x)\), which lets us conclude that a) for
the \(X_{j}\) implies a) for \(X\).

At this stage, one has only a partial converse, namely that a) for \(X\)
implies a) for the \(X_{j}\) such that \(c(x) =
c_{j}(x)\), which suffices for our purposes.\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} In
  fact, a) for \(X\) implies a) for all the \(X_{j}\) as asserted in the
  original text, but to see this one must read the proof that follows in
  detail. This implication does not seem formal at this stage. Let us
  indeed denote by an index \(J\) the conjunctions of a property a), b),
  or c) for the \(X_{j}\). It is proved in the proof below that c\_J) ⇒
  a\_J) (this is the chain of implications c′) ⇒ d) ⇒ a′)). Now one has
  tautologically a) ⇒ c), and c) ⇔ c\_J), whence a) ⇒ a\_J).}

One chooses a covering of \(X\) by opens embeddable in a regular
prescheme. Applying the preceding, one sees that one can suppose \(X\)
closed in a regular \(X'\). The reduction to \(X'\) is then immediate.

One can therefore suppose \(X\) regular, and even affine by covering
\(X\) by affine opens. That one can suppose \(F =
\tilde{M}\), where \(M\) is of finite projective dimension, will result
from the following lemma:

\textbf{Lemma.}

Let \(X\) be a regular noetherian prescheme. Let \(F\) be a coherent
\(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module. The function which to each \(x
\in X\) assigns the projective dimension of \(F_{x}\) is upper-bounded.

Indeed, let \(x \in X\) and let \(U\) be an affine open neighborhood of
\(x\). Let \(L^{\bullet}\) be a projective resolution of the module
\(F(U)\), where the \(L^{i}\) are finitely generated. By hypothesis, the
ring \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) is regular, hence the projective dimension of
\(F_{x}\) is finite; let \(d\) be that integer. Let

\[ K = \ker(L^{-d} \to L^{-d+1}). \]

The module \(K_{x}\) is free, because \(d\) is the projective dimension
of \(F_{x}\) ({[}M{]}, Ch. VI, Prop. 2.1). By
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/i/00-05-quasi-coherent-and-coherent-sheaves.html\#54-locally-free-sheaves}{EGA
0\_I 5.4.1} Errata), one deduces that the \(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Module
\(\tilde{K}\) is free on a neighborhood \(U'\) of \(x\), with \(U'
\subset U\). Choosing \(f \in \mathcal{O}_{X}(U)\) such that
\(x \in D(f) \subset U'\), one therefore has a projective resolution of
\(M_{f}\) (with \(M = F(U)\)):

\[ 0 \to K_{f} \to (L^{d-1})_{f} \to \cdots \to M_{f} \to 0, \]

which proves that the function under study is upper semi-continuous. Now
\(X\) is quasi-compact, whence the conclusion.

We henceforth suppose \(X\) affine noetherian regular and we suppose
that \(F = \tilde{M}\), where \(M\) is a finitely generated
\(A\)-module, necessarily of finite projective dimension. We shall
proceed in several steps. First, we find a condition d), equivalent to
a), and prove that it is also equivalent to c). Then, using the spectral
sequence of the preceding number, we prove d) ⇒ b). It then remains to
prove that (iii) ⇒ (ii); indeed, (i) ⇔ (ii) ⇒ (iii) follows immediately
from a) ⇔ c) ⇒ b).

Let \(x \in U\); by hypothesis \(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\) is a regular local
ring. Denoting by \(D\) the dualizing functor relative to the local ring
\(\mathcal{O}_{X,x}\), it follows from (V 2.1) that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{D H\^{}\{i{-}c(x)\}(F\_x) ≅ Ext\^{}\{d(x){-}i\}\_\{𝒪\_\{X,x\}\}(F\_x, 𝒪\_\{X,x\}),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where one has set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{d(x) = dim 𝒪\_\{X,x\} + c(x) = dim 𝒪\_\{X,x\} + codim(\{x\}̄ ∩ Y, \{x\}̄).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now \(X\) is noetherian and \(F\) is coherent, hence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{D H\^{}\{i{-}c(x)\}(F\_x) ≅ (Ext\^{}\{d(x){-}i\}\_\{𝒪\_X\}(F, 𝒪\_X))\_x.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Moreover, for a module to be zero, it is necessary and sufficient that
its dual be (cf.~editor's note (4) on page 54). For every
\(q \in \mathbb{Z}\), set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{S\_q = Supp Ext\^{}q\_\{𝒪\_X\}(F, 𝒪\_X),}
\NormalTok{S′\_q = S\_q ∩ U,    (U = X − Y),}
\NormalTok{Z\_q = S̄′\_q ∩ Y.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

From formula (2.3), it follows that a) and c) are respectively
equivalent to:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a′) for every \(q \in \mathbb{Z}\) and every \(x \in S'_{q}\), one has
  \(q + i \neq d(x)\).
\item
  c′) for every \(q \in \mathbb{Z}\) and every \(x \in S'_{q}\), if
  \(c(x) = 1\), one has \(q + i \neq d(x)\).
\end{itemize}

Here is the condition d) promised above:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \begin{enumerate}
  \def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
  \setcounter{enumi}{3}
  \tightlist
  \item
    for every \(q \in \mathbb{Z}\) and every \(y \in Z_{q}\), one has
    \(q + i \neq \dim \mathcal{O}_{X,y}\).
  \end{enumerate}
\end{itemize}

These conditions are equivalent:

a′) ⇒ c′) is trivial.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  ⇒ a′). Indeed, let \(q \in \mathbb{Z}\) and let \(x \in S'_{q}\); let
  \(y \in \bar{x} \cap Y\) which\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} In all that
    follows, closures of points are equipped with the reduced structure.}
  ``gives the codimension'', i.e.~such that
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{dim 𝒪\_\{\{x\}̄, y\} = codim(\{x\}̄ ∩ Y, \{x\}̄) = c(x).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

From the fact that \(X\) is regular at \(y\), one deduces

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{dim 𝒪\_\{X, y\} = d(x)    (cf. (2.2)).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

But \(y \in \bar{x}\), hence \(y \in Z_{q}\), whence the conclusion.

c′) ⇒ d). Let \(q \in \mathbb{Z}\) and let \(y \in Z_{q}\).
Provisionally admit that there exists \(x \in S'_{q}\) such that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{y ∈ \{x\}̄    and    dim 𝒪\_\{\{x\}̄, y\} = 1}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

(one also says that \(x\) \emph{follows} \(y\)). It follows that
\(c(x) = 1\), since \(y\) ``gives the codimension of \(\bar{x} \cap Y\)
in \(\bar{x}\)'', because \(x \notin Y\). By c′) we extract

\[ q + i \neq d(x). \]

Whence the conclusion, on noting that \(d(x) = \dim \mathcal{O}_{X, y}\)
(2.6). The admitted result is expressed in the following lemma:

\textbf{Lemma.}

Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian prescheme and let \(Y\) be a closed
subset of \(X\). Set \(U = X - Y\) and suppose that \(U\) is dense in
\(X\). For every \(y \in Y\), there exists \(x \in U\) ``which follows
it'', i.e.~such that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{y ∈ \{x\}̄    and    dim 𝒪\_\{\{x\}̄, y\} = 1.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We have applied the lemma taking for \(X\) the prescheme
\(\bar{S}'_{q}\) and for \(Y\) the part \(Y \cap \bar{S}'_{q}\).

\emph{Proof of 2.5.} --- There exists \(x \in U\) such that
\(y \in \bar{x}\); let us therefore choose \(x \in U\) such that \(y \in
\bar{x}\) and such that \(\dim \mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}, y} = r\) be
minimal. We must prove that \(r = 1\). Since we have chosen \(x\) so
that every \(z \in \operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}, y})\),
\(z \neq x\), lies in \(Y\), \({x}\) is open in
\(\operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}, y})\). Whence the
conclusion.

The second step consists in deducing b) from d).

Set \(D(Z_{q}) = {\dim \mathcal{O}_{X,y} | y \in Z_{q}}\). By d), we
know that, for every \(q \in \mathbb{Z}\), \(q + i
\notin D(Z_{q})\). One then applies VII.2.3, and sees that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Ext\^{}\{q+i\}\_Y(Ext\^{}q(F, 𝒪\_X), 𝒪\_X)    is coherent.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The initial term of the spectral sequence of the preceding number is
given by

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E₂\^{}\{p,q\} = Ext\^{}p\_Y(Ext\^{}\{{-}q\}(F, 𝒪\_X), 𝒪\_X).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

One deduces that \(E^{p,q}_{2}\) is coherent for every
\(p \in \mathbb{Z}\) and every \(q \in \mathbb{Z}\) such that \(p + q =
i\). Now there are only finitely many pairs \((p, q)\) with
\(p + q = i\), and this spectral sequence converges to
\(H^{\bullet}_{Y}(F)\), whence the conclusion.

It remains to prove that (iii) ⇒ (ii). Let us write

\[ i : U \to X \]

for the canonical immersion of \(U\) in \(X\). Taking into account the
exact homology sequence of the closed subset \(Y\) (I 2.11), one sees
that (iii) is equivalent to:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{3}
\tightlist
\item
  \(R^{i} i_{*}(F|U)\) is coherent for \(i < n\).
\end{enumerate}

Indeed, one has an exact sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{0 → ℋ\^{}0\_Y(F) → F → i\_*(F|U) → ℋ\^{}1\_Y(F) → 0.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now \(\mathcal{H}^{0}_{Y}(F)\) is a quasi-coherent subsheaf of the
coherent sheaf \(F\), hence is coherent. Therefore
\(\mathcal{H}^{1}_{Y}(F)\) is coherent if and only if \(i_{*}(F|U)\) is.
Moreover, for \(p > 0\), the exact cohomology sequence of the closed
subset \(Y\) reduces to isomorphisms

\[ R^{p} i_{*}(F|U) \xrightarrow{\sim} \mathcal{H}^{p+1}_{Y}(F). \]

We shall prove that (iv) ⇒ (ii). For this, recall (ii):

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  for every \(x \in U\) such that \(c(x) = 1\), one has
  \(prof F_{x} \geqslant n\).
\end{enumerate}

We argue by induction on \(n\).

If \(n = 0\), the two conditions are empty.

If \(n = 1\), one supposes that \(i_{*}(F|U)\) is coherent. Argue by
contradiction and suppose there exists \(x \in U\) such that
\(c(x) = 1\) and \(prof F_{x} = 0\), i.e.~\(x \in Ass F_{x}\). Let
\(y \in \bar{x} \cap Y\) such that \(\dim
\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}, y} = 1\). Set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{A = 𝒪\_\{X, y\}    and    X′ = Spec(A).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Carry out the base change \(v : X' \to X\), which is flat:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{            v′}
\NormalTok{   U′ = X′ ×\_X U  ──────►  U}
\NormalTok{        │                   │}
\NormalTok{       i′                   i}
\NormalTok{        │                   │}
\NormalTok{        ▼          v        ▼}
\NormalTok{       X′  ──────────────► X.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The morphism \(i\) is separated (since it is an immersion), and of
finite type (since it is an open immersion and \(X\) is locally
noetherian); the base change is flat, hence
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/08-ch3-01-cohomology-affine-schemes.html\#14-application-to-the-cohomology-of-arbitrary-preschemes}{EGA
III 1.4.15}) one has an isomorphism

\[ v^{*}(i_{*}(F|U)) \cong i'_{*}(v'^{*}(F|U)). \]

Let us denote by \(\mathfrak{x}\) (resp. \(\mathfrak{y}\)) the ideal of
\(A\) corresponding to \(x\) (resp. \(y\)). Set \(G =
v'^{*}(F|U)\); then \(G\) is coherent and \(\mathfrak{x} \in Ass G\), so
there exists a monomorphism \(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}}
\to G\), and consequently \(i'_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}}|U')\) is
coherent. By the choice of \(y\), \(\dim A/\mathfrak{x} =
1\), and consequently the support of \(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}}\) is
reduced to \(\bar{x} = {x} \cup {y}\), since \(\bar{x} =
\operatorname{Spec}(A/\mathfrak{x})\) as a scheme. It follows that

\[ (\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}}|U')(U') = Frac(A/\mathfrak{x}), \]

the field of fractions of \(A/\mathfrak{x}\), and

\[ i'_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}}|U')(X') = Frac(A/\mathfrak{x}). \]

But \(Frac(A/\mathfrak{x})\) is not a finitely generated \(A\)-module,
because \(\mathfrak{x}\) differs from the maximal ideal of \(A\). Whence
a contradiction.

Suppose \(n > 1\) and that the result is acquired for the \(n' < n\). By
the induction hypothesis, for every \(x \in U\) such that \(c(x) = 1\),
one has \(x \notin Ass F_{x}\). Let such an \(x\), and let
\(y \in \bar{x} \cap Y\) such that \(x\) follows \(y\),
i.e.~\(\dim \mathcal{O}_{\bar{x}, y} = 1\). Carry out the base change
\(v : \operatorname{Spec}(\mathcal{O}_{X, y})
\to X\), keeping the notation of diagram (2.7). One finds, applying (EGA
III 1.4.15), isomorphisms

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{v\^{}*(R\^{}p i\_*(F|U)) ≃ R\^{}p i′\_*(v′\^{}*(F|U)),    p ∈ ℤ.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

One thus reduces to the case where \(X\) is the spectrum of a local ring
\(A\) in which \(\mathfrak{x}\) is a prime ideal of dimension
\texttt{1}, i.e.~\(\dim A/\mathfrak{x} = 1\). Then set
\(F' = \Gamma_{Y}(F)\) and \(F'' = F/F'\). One sees that \(F_{x}
\simeq F''_{x}\) and that \(\mathfrak{y} \notin Ass F''\). Moreover
\(F'|U = 0\), whence, by the exact sequence of the \(R^{p}
i_{*}\), isomorphisms

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}p i\_*(F|U) ≃ R\^{}p i\_*(F″|U),    p ∈ ℤ.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since \(n > 1\), one deduces that neither \(\mathfrak{x}\) nor
\(\mathfrak{y}\) belongs to \(Ass F''\). Now \(\mathfrak{x},
\mathfrak{y}\) are the only prime ideals of \(A\) containing
\(\mathfrak{x}\); it follows (III 2.1) that there exists an element
\(g \in \mathfrak{x}\) which is \(M\)-regular, where one has set
\(F = \tilde{M}\), \(M = F(X)\). Whence an exact sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{0 → M ──g·→ M → N → 0,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

in which \(g\cdot\) denotes multiplication by \(g\) in \(M\). By the
exact homology sequence, one sees that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}p i\_*(Ñ|U)    is coherent for p \textless{} n − 1,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

hence, by the induction hypothesis,
\(prof(\tilde{N})_{x} \geqslant n - 1\), and therefore
\(prof F_{x} \geqslant n\). QED.

\section{3. Applications}\label{applications}

One deduces from these results a coherence condition for the higher
direct images of a coherent sheaf under a morphism that is not proper.

\textbf{Theorem.}

Let \(f : X \to Y\) be a morphism of preschemes. Suppose that \(Y\) is
locally noetherian and that \(f\) is proper. Suppose that \(X\) is
locally embeddable in a regular prescheme. Let \(n \in \mathbb{Z}\). Let
\(U\) be an open of \(X\) and let \(F\) be a coherent
\(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Module. Suppose that, for every \(x \in U\) such
that \(codim(\bar{x} \cap (X - U), \bar{x}) =
1\), one has \(prof F_{x} \geqslant n\). Then the
\(\mathcal{O}_{Y}\)-Modules \(R^{p}(f \circ g)_{*}(F)\) are coherent for
\(p <
n\), where \(g\) is the canonical immersion of \(U\) in \(X\).

Indeed, there exists a Leray spectral sequence whose abutment is
\(R^{\bullet}(f \circ g)_{*}(F)\) and whose initial term is given by

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E₂\^{}\{p,q\} = R\^{}p f\_*(R\^{}q g\_*(F)).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Moreover, there exists a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module \(G\) such
that \(G|U \simeq F\)
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/i/01-09-complements-on-quasi-coherent-sheaves.html\#94-extension-of-quasi-coherent-sheaves}{EGA
I 9.4.3}). It then follows from the preceding paragraph that condition
(iv) of page 74 is satisfied, i.e.~that \(R^{q} g_{*}(G|U)\) is coherent
for \(q < n\). One then applies the finiteness theorem of
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/10-ch3-03-finiteness-proper-morphisms.html\#32-the-finiteness-theorem-case-of-usual-schemes}{EGA
III 3.2.1} to \(f\) and to the sheaves \(R^{q} g_{*}(F)\), and finds
that \(E^{p,q}_{2}\) is coherent for \(q < n\), whence the conclusion.

\textbf{Proposition.}

Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian prescheme that is locally embeddable
in a regular prescheme. Let \(U\) be an open of \(X\) and let
\(i : U \to X\) be the canonical immersion. Let \(n \in \mathbb{Z}\).
Finally, let \(F\) be a coherent and Cohen-Macaulay
\(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Module (on \(U\)!). The following conditions are
equivalent:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\alph{enumi})}
\item
  \(R^{p} i_{*}(F)\) is coherent for \(p < n\);
\item
  for every irreducible component \(S'\) of the closure \(\bar{S}\) of
  the support \(S\) of \(F\), one has
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{codim(S′ ∩ (X − U), S′) \textgreater{} n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(G\) be a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module such that
\(G|U \simeq F\) (EGA I 9.4.3). Applying Corollary 2.3 to \(G\), one
finds that condition (a) is equivalent to:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  for every \(x \in \bar{S}\), one has \(prof F_{x} > n - c(x)\), with
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{c(x) = codim(\{x\}̄ ∩ Y, \{x\}̄).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\alph{enumi})}
\item
  ⇒ (b). Indeed, let \(S'\) be an irreducible component of \(\bar{S}\)
  and let \(s\) be its generic point. Since \(F\) is Cohen-Macaulay, one
  has \(prof F_{s} = \dim \mathcal{O}_{\bar{S}, s} = 0\). Moreover,
  \(\bar{s} = S'\), whence the conclusion.
\item
  ⇒ (a). Let \(x \in \bar{S}\) and let \(S'\) be an irreducible
  component of \(\bar{S}\) such that \(x \in S'\). Let \(s\) be the
  generic point of \(S'\). Since \(F\) is Cohen-Macaulay, one knows that
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof F\_x = dim 𝒪\_\{\{s\}̄, x\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

If \(c(x) = +\infty\), there is nothing to prove. Otherwise, there
exists \(y \in Y \cap \bar{x}\) such that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{c(x) = dim 𝒪\_\{\{x\}̄, y\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now \(\mathcal{O}_{X, y}\) is a quotient of a regular local ring by
hypothesis, hence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{dim 𝒪\_\{\{s\}̄, y\} = dim 𝒪\_\{\{s\}̄, x\} + dim 𝒪\_\{\{x\}̄, y\} \textgreater{} n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

QED.

\section{Bibliography}\label{bibliography-2}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  {[}M{]} H. Cartan and S. Eilenberg, \emph{Homological Algebra},
  Princeton Math. Series vol.~19, Princeton University Press, 1956.
\end{enumerate}

VIII.1.4. \\
double complexe / bicomplexe & double complex / bicomplex & Source uses
both interchangeably; preserved. \\
aboutissement & abutment & Standard for spectral sequences. \\
terme initial & initial term & Used for \(E_{2}\) page. \\
localement immergeable dans un préschéma régulier & locally embeddable
in a regular prescheme & Standing hypothesis of Theorem VIII.2.1. \\
\(c(x) = codim(\bar{x} \cap Y, \bar{x})\) & \(c(x)\) as written &
Preserved verbatim from (2.1); closures are reduced (cf.~N.D.E.). \\
profondeur (\(prof F_{x}\)) & depth (\(prof F_{x}\)) & Per glossary; the
source uses \texttt{prof}. \\
« x suit y » & ``\(x\) follows \(y\)'' & Translator keeps quotation
marks since the source flags it; Lemma VIII.2.5. \\
sous-faisceau quasi-cohérent & quasi-coherent subsheaf & Standard. \\
de Cohen-Macaulay (sur U !) & Cohen-Macaulay (on \(U\)!) & Exclamation
preserved; the parenthetical insists \(F\) is Cohen-Macaulay on the open
\(U\), not on a global ambient. \\
condition (a), (b), (c), (d) & condition (a), (b), (c), (d) & Lowercase
Latin letters in this Exposé (not Roman); (i)--(iv) in Corollary 2.3
stay Roman, per the source. \\
\end{longtable}
}

Note on the \(\Gamma Z\)/\(\mathcal{H}^{\bullet}_{Y}\) typographic
convention: in this Exposé, sheafified local cohomology is rendered
\(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{Y}(F)\) (script-H) and global sections with support
remain \(H^{i}_{Y}(X, F)\). The underlined section functor of the source
is, when it appears, written \(\Gamma_{Y}\) in line with the volume-wide
convention recorded in the introduction.

\chapter{Exposé IX. Algebraic geometry and formal
geometry}\label{exposuxe9-ix.-algebraic-geometry-and-formal-geometry}

The goal of this Exposé is to generalize, to the case of a morphism that
is not proper, Theorems 3.4.2 and 4.1.5 of EGA III.

\section{1. The comparison theorem}\label{the-comparison-theorem}

Let \(f: X \to X'\) be a separated morphism of preschemes of finite
type. Suppose that \(X\) is locally noetherian. Let \(Y'\) be a closed
subset of \(X'\) and let \(Y = f^{-1}(Y')\).

Let \(\hat{X}\) and \(\hat{X}'\) be the formal completions of \(X\) and
\(X'\) along \(Y\) and \(Y'\). Let \(\hat{f}\) be the morphism deduced
from \(f\) by passing to the completions.

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{    X ◀───── Y               X̂ ──j──▶  X}
\NormalTok{    │                        │           │}
\NormalTok{    │ f                      │ f̂         │ f}
\NormalTok{    ▼                        ▼           ▼}
\NormalTok{    X′ ◀──── Y′      ,       X̂′ ──i──▶ X′.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We denote by \(j\) (resp. \(i\)) the homomorphism from \(\hat{X}\) into
\(X\) (resp. from \(\hat{X}'\) into \(X'\)). It is known that \(i\) and
\(j\) are flat.

Let \(I'\) be an ideal of definition of \(Y'\), and let
\(J = f*(I')\cdot \mathcal{O}_{X}\); this is an ideal of definition of
\(Y\). One therefore has:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X̂′ = (Y′, lim\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} 𝒪\_\{X′\}/I′\^{}\{k+1\}),    X̂ = (Y, lim\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} 𝒪\_X/J\^{}\{k+1\}).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

For every \(k \in \mathbb{N}\), set:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Y′\_k = (Y′, 𝒪\_\{X′\}/I′\^{}\{k+1\}),    Y\_k = (Y, 𝒪\_X/J\^{}\{k+1\}).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(F\) be a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module. For every
\(k \in \mathbb{N}\), we set:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{F\_k = F/J\^{}\{k+1\}F,    F̂ = j*(F) ≃ lim\_\{k\} F\_k.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

If we set:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}i f\_*(F)\^{}∧ = lim\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} (R\^{}i f\_*(F) ⊗\_\{𝒪\_\{X′\}\} 𝒪\_\{Y′\_k\}),    i ∈ ℤ,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

one has a natural homomorphism:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{r\_i: i*(R\^{}i f\_*(F)) → R\^{}i f\_*(F)\^{}∧,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

which is an isomorphism when \(R^{i} f_{*}(F)\) is coherent.

As is explained in
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/11-ch3-04-fundamental-theorem-proper-morphisms.html\#41-the-fundamental-theorem}{EGA
III 4.1.1}, one has a commutative diagram:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{                       ρ\_i}
\NormalTok{   i*(R\^{}i f\_*(F))  ──────▶  R\^{}i f̂\_*(F̂)}
\NormalTok{        │                       │}
\NormalTok{     r\_i│                       │ψ\_i}
\NormalTok{        ▼          ϕ\_i          ▼}
\NormalTok{   R\^{}i f\_*(F)\^{}∧  ──────▶  lim\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} R\^{}i f\_*(F\_k).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

In loc. cit. one finds a commutative diagram, since one knows that
\(R^{i} f_{*}(F)\) is coherent, and one identifies the source and target
of (1.6). In our case, \(R^{i} f_{*}(F)\) will be coherent only for
certain values of \(i\), for which we shall study (1.7).

Consider the graded ring

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{S = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} I′\^{}k,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

and the graded \(S\)-Module:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} R\^{}i f\_*(J\^{}k F),    i ∈ ℤ,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

whose \(S\)-Module structure is defined as follows.

The sheaf \(R^{i} f_{*}(J^{k} F)\) is associated with the presheaf
which, to every affine open \(U'\) of \(X'\), associates:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i(f⁻¹(U′), J\^{}k F | f⁻¹(U′)).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(U'\) then be an affine open of \(X'\), set

\[ U = f^{-1}(U'), \]

and let \(x' \in I'^{m}(U')\). Let \(x\) be the image of \(x'\) in
\(J^{m}(U)\). The homothety of ratio \(x\) on \(F | U\) maps
\(J^{k} F | U\) into \(J^{k+m} F | U\), whence, by functoriality, a
morphism:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{μ\^{}i\_\{x′, k\}(U′): H\^{}i(U, J\^{}k F | U) → H\^{}i(U, J\^{}\{k+m\} F | U),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

defined for every \(i \in \mathbb{Z}\) and every \(k \in \mathbb{N}\),
which gives, by passing to the associated sheaf, the graded \(S\)-Module
structure on \(H^{i}\).

\textbf{Theorem.}

Let \(n\) be an integer. Suppose that the graded \(S\)-Module \(H^{i}\)
is of finite type for \(i = n - 1\) and \(i = n\). Then:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\arabic{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{-1}
\item
  \(r_{n}\) and \(r_{n-1}\) are isomorphisms, and
  \(R^{i} \hat{f}_{*}(\hat{F})\) is coherent for \(i = n - 1\);
\item
  for \(i = n - 1\), \(\rho_{i}\), \(\varphi_{i}\), and \(\psi_{i}\) are
  topological isomorphisms (in particular, the filtration defined on
  \(R^{n-1} f_{*}(F)\) by the kernels of the homomorphisms
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}\{n−1\} f\_*(F) → R\^{}\{n−1\} f\_*(F\_k)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is \(I'\)-good);

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\arabic{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\item
  for \(i = n\), \(\rho_{i}\), \(\varphi_{i}\), and \(\psi_{i}\) are
  monomorphisms; furthermore, the filtration on \(R^{n}
  f_{*}(F)\) is \(I'\)-good and \(\psi_{n}\) is an isomorphism;
\item
  the projective system of the \(R^{i} f_{*}(F_{k})\) satisfies, for
  \(i = n - 2, n - 1\), the uniform Mittag-Leffler condition, i.e.~there
  exists a fixed integer \(k \geqslant 0\) such that, for every
  \(p \geqslant 0\) and every \(p'
  \geqslant p + k\), one has:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Im[R\^{}i f\_*(F\_\{p′\}) → R\^{}i f\_*(F\_p)] = Im[R\^{}i f\_*(F\_\{p+k\}) → R\^{}i f\_*(F\_p)].}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Proceeding as in
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/11-ch3-04-fundamental-theorem-proper-morphisms.html\#41-the-fundamental-theorem}{EGA
III 4.1.8}, it is easy to reduce to the case where \(X'\) is the
spectrum of a noetherian ring \(A\). In this case, one knows that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}i f\_*(F) = H\^{}i(X, F)\^{}\textasciitilde{}     (cf. 1.10).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(I\) be the ideal of \(A\) such that \(\tilde{I} = I'\), and let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{S = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} I\^{}k,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} H\^{}i(X, J\^{}k F),    i ∈ ℤ,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(H^{i}\) is equipped with the graded \(S\)-module structure
defined by 1.11, where one has taken \(U' = X'\).

The proof is modelled on that of
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/11-ch3-04-fundamental-theorem-proper-morphisms.html\#41-the-fundamental-theorem}{EGA
III 4.1.5}; let us give a summary.

We work on \(\varphi_{i}\) and \(\psi_{i}\), which correspond to
homomorphisms of modules:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{                              H\^{}i(X̂, F̂)}
\NormalTok{                                  │}
\NormalTok{                                  │ψ\_i}
\NormalTok{                  ϕ\_i             ▼}
\NormalTok{   H\^{}i(X, F)\^{}∧  ──────▶  lim\_\{k\} H\^{}i(X, F\_k).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\alph{enumi})}
\tightlist
\item
  We assume only that \(H^{i}\) is a graded \(S\)-module of finite type.
  We deduce that the filtration defined on \(H^{i}(X, F)\) by the
  modules:
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}i\_k = ker(H\^{}i(X, F) → H\^{}i(X, F\_k))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is \(I\)-good. For this, we use the cohomology exact sequence:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i(X, J\^{}\{k+1\} F) → H\^{}i(X, F) → H\^{}i(X, F\_k),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

which shows that the graded \(S\)-module
\(\bigoplus_{k \in \mathbb{N}} R^{i}_{k}\) is a quotient of the graded
\(S\)-submodule

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} H\^{}i(X, J\^{}\{k+1\} F)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

of \(H^{i}\), hence is of finite type, since \(S\) is noetherian. Whence
this first point.

Set:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{M\^{}i = H\^{}i(X, F),    H\^{}i\_k = H\^{}i(X, F\_k).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

One has a commutative diagram:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{                     s\_i}
\NormalTok{   H\^{}i(X, F)\^{}∧  ─────────▶  lim\_\{k\} (M\^{}i / R\^{}i\_k)}
\NormalTok{        \textbackslash{}                         │}
\NormalTok{         \textbackslash{}                        │}
\NormalTok{          \textbackslash{} ϕ\_i                   │ t\_i}
\NormalTok{           \textbackslash{}                      ▼}
\NormalTok{            ──────────────▶  lim\_\{k\} H\^{}i\_k,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

in which \(s_{i}\) is an isomorphism; indeed, the filtration of
\(H^{i}(X, F)\) is \(I\)-good. Moreover, \(t_{i}\) is a monomorphism;
indeed, the functor \texttt{lim} is left exact, and, for every
\(k \geqslant 0\), the natural morphism \(M^{i} /
R^{i}_{k} \to H^{i}_{k}\) is a monomorphism, by definition of
\(R^{i}_{k}\).

To study the surjectivity of \(t_{i}\), we introduce:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Q\^{}i\_k = coker(H\^{}i(X, F) → H\^{}i(X, F\_k)),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

whence a projective system of exact sequences:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{0 → R\^{}i\_k → M\^{}i → H\^{}i\_k → Q\^{}i\_k → 0.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Using the cohomology exact sequence:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i(X, F) → H\^{}i(X, F\_k) → H\^{}\{i+1\}(X, J\^{}\{k+1\} F),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

one sees that the graded \(S\)-module

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Q\^{}i = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} Q\^{}i\_k}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is a graded \(S\)-submodule of \(H^{i+1}\). Moreover, for every
\(k \geqslant 0\), one has:

\[ I^{k+1} Q^{i}_{k} = 0, \]

since \(Q^{i}_{k}\) is the image of \(H^{i}_{k}\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  We assume only that \(H^{i+1}\) is of finite type, and we focus on
  \(t_{i}\) (forgetting \(s_{i}\)). Since \(S\) is noetherian, \(Q^{i}\)
  is of finite type; since \(I^{k+1} Q^{i}_{k}\) vanishes, we find that
  there exist an integer \(r
  \geqslant 0\) and an integer \(k_{0} \geqslant 0\) such that
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{I\^{}r Q\^{}i\_k = 0    for k ⩾ k₀.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

It follows that the projective system \((Q^{i}_{k})_{k \in \mathbb{N}}\)
is essentially zero, and hence the projective system
\((H^{i}_{k})_{k \in \mathbb{N}}\) satisfies the uniform Mittag-Leffler
condition. From the exact sequence (1.22) one deduces the exact sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{0 → M\^{}i / R\^{}i\_k → H\^{}i\_k → Q\^{}i\_k → 0,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

whence the exact sequence:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{0 → lim\_\{k\} M\^{}i / R\^{}i\_k  ──t\_i──▶  lim\_\{k\} H\^{}i\_k → lim\_\{k\} Q\^{}i\_k.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now the projective system \((Q^{i}_{k})_{k \in \mathbb{N}}\) is
essentially zero, hence \(t_{i}\) is an isomorphism.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  Let us prove that, if \(H^{i}\) is of finite type, then \(\psi_{i}\)
  is an isomorphism. It suffices to apply
  \href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/06-ch0-13-projective-limits-homological-algebra.html\#133-application-cohomology-of-a-projective-limit-of-sheaves}{EGA
  0\_III 13.3.1}, taking as a basis of opens of \(X\) the affine opens.
  This is legitimate;
\end{enumerate}

indeed, by (b), the projective system
\((H^{i-1}_{k})_{k \in \mathbb{N}}\) satisfies the Mittag-Leffler
condition.

The theorem follows formally from (a), (b), and (c). One will note that,
in fact, the proof uses, at each step, the finiteness of \(H^{i}\) only
for a single value of \(i\).

Let us give some examples in which the hypothesis of Theorem 1.1 is
satisfied.

\textbf{Corollary.}

Suppose that \(I'\) is generated by a section \(t'\) of
\(\mathcal{O}_{X'}\), and denote by \(t\) the corresponding section of
\(\mathcal{O}_{X}\). Let \(F\) be a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-module
and let \(n\) be an integer. Suppose that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  \(t\) is \(F\)-regular (i.e.~the homothety of ratio \(t\) on \(F\) is
  a monomorphism).
\item
  \(R^{i} f_{*}(F)\) is coherent for \(i = n - 1\) and \(i = n\).
\end{enumerate}

Then the hypothesis of Theorem 1.1 is satisfied.

Indeed, one observes that multiplication by \(t^{k}\) defines an
isomorphism \(F \xrightarrow{\sim} J^{k} F\), and one deduces that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i ≃ R\^{}i f\_*(F) ⊗\_\{𝒪\_\{X′\}\} 𝒪\_\{X′\}[T],}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(T\) is an indeterminate. Whence the conclusion.

\textbf{Corollary.}

Suppose that \(X' = \operatorname{Spec}(A)\), where \(A\) is a
noetherian ring separated and complete for the \(I\)-adic topology.
Suppose that the \(S\)-module \(H^{i}\) is of finite type for
\(i = n - 1\) and \(i = n\) (cf.~1.14 and 1.15). Then the hypotheses of
Theorem 1.1 are satisfied, and one finds a commutative diagram of
isomorphisms:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{                    ρ′\_i}
\NormalTok{   H\^{}i(X, F)  ─────────────▶  H\^{}i(X̂, F̂)}
\NormalTok{         \textbackslash{}                      /}
\NormalTok{          \textbackslash{}                    /}
\NormalTok{           \textbackslash{} ϕ′\_i         ψ\_i /}
\NormalTok{            \textbackslash{}                /}
\NormalTok{             ▼              ▼}
\NormalTok{              lim\_\{k\} H\^{}i(X, F\_k)              for i = n − 1.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} In the same vein, see the article by Chow (Chow
  W.-L., ``Formal functions on homogeneous spaces'', \emph{Invent.
  Math.} \textbf{86} (1986), no. 1, pp.~115--130). The author proves the
  following result. Let \(X\) be an algebraic variety over a field,
  homogeneous under an algebraic group \(G\), and let \(Z\) be a
  complete subvariety of \(X\) of dimension \texttt{\textgreater{}\ 0}.
  Suppose that \(Z\) generates \(X\) in the following sense: given
  \(p \in Z\), let \(\Gamma_{p}\) be the set of elements of \(G\)
  sending \(p\) into \(Z\). One then says that \(Z\) generates if the
  group generated by the connected component of \texttt{1} of
  \(\Gamma_{p}\) is the whole of \(G\). In this case, every formal
  rational function on \(X\) along \(Z\) is algebraic; compare with the
  results of Hironaka and Matsumura cited in editor's note (3) p.~138.
  In the line of the techniques introduced by these authors, let us
  point out the very pretty algebraization result due to Gieseker
  (Gieseker D., ``On two theorems of Griffiths about embeddings with
  ample normal bundle'', \emph{Amer. J. Math.} \textbf{99} (1977), no.
  6, pp.~1137--1150, Theorems 4.1 and 4.2). Let \(X\) be a connected
  projective variety of dimension \texttt{\textgreater{}\ 0}, locally a
  complete intersection (over an algebraically closed field). Suppose
  one has two embeddings of \(X\) into smooth projective varieties
  \texttt{Y,\ W}. Then, if the formal completions of \(X\) in \(Y\) and
  \(W\) are equivalent, there exists a scheme \(U\) containing \(X\) (as
  a closed subscheme) which embeds into \(Y\) and \(W\) as an étale
  neighborhood of \(X\) in \(Y\) and \(W\). In other words, formally
  equivalent entails étale-equivalent. See also the article of Faltings
  (Faltings G., ``Formale Geometrie und homogene Räume'', \emph{Invent.
  Math.} \textbf{64} (1981), pp.~123--165).}

One simply notes that \(H^{i}(X, F)\) is of finite type, hence
isomorphic to its completion. One obtains (1.1) by transcribing the
diagram of Modules (1.7) into the category of \(A\)-modules, and
replacing the left vertical by \(H^{i}(X,
F)\).

\textbf{Proposition.}

Let \(A\) be a noetherian ring. Let \(t \in A\) and suppose that \(A\)
is separated and complete for the \texttt{(tA)}-adic topology. Set:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X′ = Spec(A),    Y′ = V(t),    I = (tA).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(T\) be a closed subset of \(X'\); set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X = X′ − T,    Y = Y′ ∩ X = Y′ − (Y′ ∩ T).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(F\) be a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module. Finally, let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{T′ = \{x ∈ X′ | codim(\{x\} ∩ T, \{x\}) = 1\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Suppose that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\item
  \(t\) is \(F\)-regular,
\item
  \(prof_{T'}(F) \geqslant n + 1\),
\item
  \(A\) is a quotient of a regular noetherian ring.
\end{enumerate}

Then, in diagram (1.1), the morphisms \(\rho'_{i}\), \(\varphi'_{i}\),
and \(\psi_{i}\) are isomorphisms for \(i < n\) and monomorphisms for
\(i = n\). Moreover \(\psi_{n}\) is an isomorphism.

By virtue of 1.3 and 1.2, it suffices to prove that \(R^{i} f_{*}(F)\)
is coherent for \(i \leqslant n\), which follows from the finiteness
theorem 2.1.\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} The ``finiteness theorem 2.1''
  referenced here is the finiteness theorem of Exposé VIII (VIII 2.3),
  not the Theorem 2.1 of the present Exposé; the source's local
  cross-reference is to the cohomological finiteness statement on which
  the existence theorem is built.}

In particular:

\textbf{Example.}

One will apply 1.4 when \(A\) is a local ring and \(t\) belongs to the
radical \(r(A)\) of \(A\). One will then take \(T =
{r(A)}\). In this case, for \(n = 1\), one obtains the following
statement:

If \(A\) is noetherian, separated and complete for the \(t\)-adic
topology, and a quotient of a regular ring (for example, if \(A\) is
complete), if moreover \(t\) is \(F\)-regular and if
\(prof F_{x} \geqslant 2\) for every \(x \in
\operatorname{Spec}(A)\) such that \(\dim A/x = 1\), then the natural
homomorphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Γ(X, F) → Γ(X̂, F̂)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is an isomorphism.

Indeed, keeping the notation of 1.4, one has \(T = {r(A)}\), and formula
(1.33) says that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{T′ = \{x ∈ Spec(A) | dim A/x = 1\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{2. The existence theorem}\label{the-existence-theorem}

Let us first state
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/10-ch3-03-finiteness-proper-morphisms.html\#34-the-finiteness-theorem-case-of-formal-schemes}{EGA
III 3.4.2} in a slightly more general form.

Let \(f: X \to X'\) be an adic morphism\footnote{This hypothesis is not
  essential; cf.~XII, p.~118.} of formal preschemes, with \(X'\)
noetherian. Let \(I'\) be an ideal of definition of \(X'\); since \(f\)
is adic, \(f*I' = J\) is\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} By definition itself,
  cf.
  \href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/i/01-10-formal-schemes.html\#1012-adic-morphisms-of-formal-preschemes}{EGA
  I 10.12.1}.} an ideal of definition of \(X\).

For every \(n \in \mathbb{N}\), set

\[ X_{n} = (X, \mathcal{O}_{X}/J^{n+1}); \]

this is an ordinary prescheme having the same underlying topological
space as \(X\).

Let \(F\) be a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module. For every
\(k \in \mathbb{N}\), the \(\mathcal{O}_{X_{k}}\)-Modules

\[ F_{k} = F/J^{k+1} F \]

are coherent. For every \(i\), one has a homomorphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ψ\_i: R\^{}i f\_*(F) → lim\_\{k\} R\^{}i f\_*(F\_k),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

deduced by functoriality from the natural homomorphism:

\[ F \to F_{k}. \]

Set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{S = gr\_\{I′\} 𝒪\_\{X′\} = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} I′\^{}k / I′\^{}\{k+1\},}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{gr\_J(F) = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} J\^{}k F / J\^{}\{k+1\} F,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{K\^{}i = R\^{}i f\_*(gr\_J(F)) = ⨁\_\{k ∈ ℕ\} R\^{}i f\_*(J\^{}k F / J\^{}\{k+1\} F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

It is clear that \(K^{i}\) is equipped with a graded \(S\)-Module
structure.

\textbf{Theorem.}

Suppose that \(K^{i}\) is a graded \(S\)-Module of finite type for
\(i = n - 1\), \(i = n\), \(i = n + 1\). Then:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  \(R^{n} f_{*}(F)\) is coherent.
\item
  The homomorphism \(\psi_{n}\) (2.3) is a topological isomorphism. The
  natural filtration of the right-hand side of (2.3) is \(I'\)-good.
\item
  The projective system of the \(R^{n} f_{*}(F_{k})\) satisfies the
  uniform Mittag-Leffler condition.
\end{enumerate}

The proof is very easy from
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/06-ch0-13-projective-limits-homological-algebra.html\#137-derived-functors-of-a-projective-limit-of-arguments}{EGA
0\_III 13.7.7} (cf.~EGA III 3.4.2), provided one corrects the text on
page 78 as indicated in
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/09-ch3-02-cohomology-projective-morphisms.html\#2-cohomological-study-of-projective-morphisms}{EGA
III 2}, Err\_III 24).

\textbf{Theorem.}

\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} Numerous algebraization statements have been
  obtained since, not to mention those cited below; cf.~the articles of
  Faltings or of Mme Raynaud cited in editor's notes (22) p.~155 and (7)
  p.~203 respectively. One has in mind in particular the results of
  Artin (see notably Artin M., ``Algebraization of formal moduli. I'',
  in \emph{Global Analysis (Papers in Honor of K. Kodaira)}, Univ. Tokyo
  Press, Tokyo, 1969, pp.~21--71), but also the recent algebraicity
  results for leaves of foliations; see notably Bost J.-B., ``Algebraic
  leaves of algebraic foliations over number fields'', \emph{Publ. Math.
  Inst. Hautes Études Sci.} \textbf{93} (2001), pp.~161--221, and
  Chambert-Loir A., ``Théorèmes d'algébricité en géométrie diophantienne
  (d'après J.-B. Bost, Y. André, D. \& G. Chudnovsky)'', in
  \emph{Séminaire Bourbaki, Vol. 2000/2001}, \emph{Astérisque},
  vol.~282, Société mathématique de France, Paris, 2002, Exp. 886,
  pp.~175--209, and the references cited therein. In particular, one
  will find in these two articles discussions of the link between
  algebraization questions and the theory of diophantine approximation.}

Let \(A\) be a noetherian adic ring and let \(I\) be an ideal of
definition of \(A\). Let \(T\) be a closed subset of \(X' =
\operatorname{Spec}(A)\). Suppose that \(I\) is generated by a
\(t \in A\). Take up the notation 1.31, 1.32, and 1.33. Let \(F\) be a
coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\)-Module. Set

\[ F_{0} = F/J F, \]

where \(J = t\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\) is an ideal of definition of
\(\hat{X}\). Suppose that \(A\) is a quotient of a regular noetherian
ring and that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\arabic{enumi})}
\item
  \(t\) is \(F\)-regular,
\item
  \(prof_{T'} F_{0} \geqslant 2\).
\end{enumerate}

Then there exists a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module \(\tilde{F}\)
such that \(\tilde{F}^{\wedge} \simeq F\).

It suffices to prove that \(\hat{f}_{*}(F)\) is a coherent
\(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}'}\)-Module, where \(\hat{f}: \hat{X} \to
\hat{X}'\) is the morphism of formal preschemes deduced from the
injection of \(X\) into \(X'\) by completion with respect to \(t\).
Indeed, \(A\) is separated and complete for the \(t\)-adic topology, so
there will exist an \(A\)-module \(F'\) whose completion will be
isomorphic to \(\hat{f}_{*}(F)\). Since \(X\) is an open of \(X'\), one
will be able to take \(\tilde{F} =
\tilde{F}' | X\).

It remains to show that 2.1 is applicable to the morphism of formal
preschemes \(\hat{f}\) and to \(F\). Now, by hypothesis (1), for every
\(k \in \mathbb{N}\) one has an isomorphism:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{J\^{}k F / J\^{}\{k+1\} F → F/J F,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

whence it follows that the hypothesis of 2.1 will be satisfied if one
knows that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}i f\_*(F₀) is coherent for i ⩽ 1.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now this follows from (2) and from the finiteness theorem
2.1.\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} The ``finiteness theorem 2.1'' referenced
  here is the finiteness theorem of Exposé VIII (VIII 2.3), not the
  Theorem 2.1 of the present Exposé; the source's local cross-reference
  is to the cohomological finiteness statement on which the existence
  theorem is built.} Whence the conclusion.

It remains to specialize this statement by supposing that \(A\) is a
local ring.

\textbf{Corollary.}

Let \(A\) be a noetherian local ring and let \(t \in r(A)\) be an
element of the radical of \(A\). Suppose that \(A\) is separated and
complete for the \(t\)-adic topology and, moreover, a quotient of a
regular ring (for example, suppose that \(A\) is a complete noetherian
local ring). Set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X′ = Spec A,    T = \{r(A)\},}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

and take up the notation (1.31), (1.32), and (1.33). Let \(F\) be an
\(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\)-Module. Suppose that:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\arabic{enumi})}
\item
  \(t\) is \(F\)-regular,
\item
  \(prof_{T'} F_{0} \geqslant 2\), with \(F_{0} = F/J F\) and
  \(J = t\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\).
\end{enumerate}

Then there exists a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module \(\tilde{F}\)
such that \(\tilde{F}^{\wedge} \simeq F\).

Note that here \(T'\) is the set of prime ideals \(p\) of \(A\) such
that \(\dim A/p = 1\).

\chapter{Exposé X. Application to the fundamental
group}\label{exposuxe9-x.-application-to-the-fundamental-group}

Throughout this Exposé, \(X\) will denote a locally noetherian
prescheme, \(Y\) a closed part of \(X\), \(U\) a variable open
neighborhood of \(Y\) in \(X\), and \(\hat{X}\) the formal completion of
\(X\) along \(Y\)
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/i/01-10-formal-schemes.html\#108-formal-completion-of-a-prescheme-along-a-closed-subset}{EGA
I 10.8}). For every prescheme \(Z\), we denote by \(\hat{E}t(Z)\) the
category of étale coverings of \(Z\), and by \(L(Z)\) the category of
locally free coherent Modules on \(Z\).

\section{\texorpdfstring{1. Comparison of \(\hat{E}t(\hat{X})\) and
\(\hat{E}t(Y)\)}{1. Comparison of \textbackslash hat\{E\}t(\textbackslash hat\{X\}) and \textbackslash hat\{E\}t(Y)}}\label{comparison-of-hatethatx-and-hatety}

Let \(I\) be an ideal of definition of \(Y\) in \(X\). Set, for every
\(n \in \mathbb{N}\), \(Y_{n} = (Y,
(\mathcal{O}_{X}/I^{n+1})|Y)\). The \(Y_{n}\) form a direct system of
ordinary preschemes, or also of formal preschemes, by equipping the
structure sheaves with the discrete topology. One knows
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/i/01-10-formal-schemes.html\#106-formal-preschemes-as-inductive-limits}{EGA
I 10.6.2}) that \(\hat{X}\) is the direct limit, in the category of
formal preschemes, of the direct system of the \(Y_{n}\). One also knows
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/i/01-10-formal-schemes.html\#1013-morphisms-of-finite-type}{EGA
I 10.13}) that to give a formal \(\hat{X}\)-prescheme of finite type
\(R\) is the same as to give a direct system of \(Y_{n}\)-preschemes
\(R_{n}\) of finite type, such that
\(R_{n} \simeq (R_{n+1}) \times_{(Y_{n+1})} (Y_{n})\). Moreover, in
order that \(R\) be an étale covering of \(\hat{X}\), it is necessary
and sufficient that for every \(n\), \(R_{n}\) be an étale covering of
\(Y_{n}\). This said, it is easy to see that nilpotent elements do not
matter for étale coverings (SGA 1 8.3), that is, that the base-change
functor

\[ \hat{E}t(Y_{n+1}) \longrightarrow \hat{E}t(Y_{n}) \]

is an equivalence of categories for every \(n \in \mathbb{N}\). Hence:

\textbf{Proposition.}

With the notation introduced above, the natural functor
\(\hat{E}t(\hat{X}) \to \hat{E}t(Y)\) is an equivalence of categories
(cf.~SGA 1 8.4).

\section{\texorpdfstring{2. Comparison of \(\hat{E}t(Y)\) and
\(\hat{E}t(U)\), for \(U\)
variable}{2. Comparison of \textbackslash hat\{E\}t(Y) and \textbackslash hat\{E\}t(U), for U variable}}\label{comparison-of-hatety-and-hatetu-for-u-variable}

We shall introduce two conditions from which the announced comparison
theorem will follow easily. Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian prescheme
and let \(Y\) be a closed part of \(X\). One says that the pair
\((X, Y)\) satisfies the \emph{Lefschetz condition}, written
\(Lef(X, Y)\), if, for every open \(U\) of \(X\) containing \(Y\) and
every locally free coherent sheaf \(E\) on \(U\), the natural
homomorphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Γ(U, E) ⟶ Γ(X̂, Ê)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is an isomorphism.

One says that the pair \((X, Y)\) satisfies the \emph{effective
Lefschetz condition}, written \(Leff(X, Y)\), if \(Lef(X, Y)\) holds and
if, moreover, for every locally free coherent sheaf \(E\) on
\(\hat{X}\), there exist an open neighborhood \(U\) of \(Y\), a locally
free coherent sheaf \(E\) on \(U\), and an isomorphism
\(\hat{E} \simeq E\).

These conditions are satisfied in two important examples:

\textbf{Example.}\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} One can slightly improve (i):
  see Mme Raynaud (Raynaud M., ``Théorèmes de Lefschetz en cohomologie
  des faisceaux cohérents et en cohomologie étale. Application au groupe
  fondamental'', \emph{Ann. Sci. Éc. Norm. Sup.} (4) \textbf{7} (1974),
  pp.~29--52, corollaries I.1.4 and I.5); the condition (ii) can be
  improved so as to get rid of the depth conditions along \(Y\) (see
  Theorem 3.3 of loc. cit. for a precise statement). The proof of this
  last point is very technical, the article cited above giving only
  indications of proof and referring to a detailed earlier version
  published in the \emph{Bulletin de la Société mathématique de France}.}

Let \(A\) be a noetherian ring and let \(t \in \mathfrak{r}(A)\) be an
\(A\)-regular element belonging to the radical \(\mathfrak{r}(A)\) of
\(A\). Suppose that \(A\) is a quotient of a regular local ring and that
\(A\) is complete for the \(t\)-adic topology (for example \(A\)
complete for the \(\mathfrak{r}(A)\)-adic topology). Set
\(X' = \operatorname{Spec}(A)\) and \(Y' = V(t)\); further, set
\(x = \mathfrak{r}(A)\) and \(X = X' - {x}\), \(Y = Y' - {x}\). So \(X\)
is open in \(X'\) and \(Y =
X \cap Y'\). Then:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  If, for every prime ideal \(\mathfrak{p}\) of \(A\) such that
  \(\dim A/\mathfrak{p} = 1\) (i.e.~for every closed point of \(X\)),
  one has \(prof A_{\mathfrak{p}} \geqslant 2\), then \(Lef(X, Y)\)
  holds;
\item
  if, moreover, for every prime ideal \(\mathfrak{p}\) of \(A\) such
  that \(t \in \mathfrak{p}\) and \(\dim A/\mathfrak{p} =
  1\) (i.e.~for every closed point of \(Y\)), one has
  \(prof A_{\mathfrak{p}} \geqslant 3\), then \(Leff(X, Y)\) holds.
\end{enumerate}

Let us first show that, for every open neighborhood \(U\) of \(Y\) in
\(X\), the complement of \(U\) in \(X\) is a union of a finite number of
closed points (in \(X\)). Note that \(U\) is open in \(X\), hence in
\(X'\), so \(Z' = X' - U\) is closed.

Let \(I\) be an ideal of definition of \(Z'\); it suffices to prove that
\(A/I\) is of dimension \texttt{1}. But \(Z' \cap Y' = {x}\), so
\(A/(I + (t))\) is artinian, whence the conclusion by the
``Hauptidealsatz''.

The first hypothesis is equivalent to: ``for every prime ideal
\(\mathfrak{p}\) of \(A\), \(\mathfrak{p} \neq
\mathfrak{r}(A)\), one has
\(prof A_{\mathfrak{p}} \geqslant 3 - \dim A/\mathfrak{p}\)''. Indeed,
\(A\) is a quotient of a regular ring, so one may apply VIII 2.3 to the
prescheme \(X'\), to the closed part \({x}\), and to the coherent sheaf
\(\mathcal{O}_{X'}\), observing that
\(c(\mathfrak{p}) = \dim(A/\mathfrak{p})\) for
\(\mathfrak{p} \in U = X' - {x}\) (since \(x\) is the closed point of
\(X'\)).

Let \(U\) be an open neighborhood of \(Y\) in \(X\) and let \(E\) be a
locally free \(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-module. Set \(Z = X - U\) and let
\(u: U \to X\) be the canonical immersion. We shall first prove that
\(u_{*}(E)\) is a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module, or what amounts
to the same, that \(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{Z}(E')\) is coherent for
\(i = 0, 1\), where \(E'\) is a coherent extension of \(E\) to \(X\). To
do this, one applies Theorem VIII 2.1 to the prescheme \(X\), to the
closed part \(Z\), and to the coherent sheaf \(E'\). It suffices to
verify that for every point \(\mathfrak{p} \in U\) such that
\(c(\mathfrak{p}) = 1\), one has \(prof E'_{\mathfrak{p}} \geqslant 1\),
where we have set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{c(𝔭) = codim(\{𝔭\}̄ ∩ Z, \{𝔭\}̄).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now if \(\mathfrak{p} \in U\) and \(c(\mathfrak{p}) = 1\), denoting
again by \(\mathfrak{p}\) the ideal of \(A\) corresponding to
\(\mathfrak{p}\), one sees that \(\dim A/\mathfrak{p} = 2\), since the
complement of \(U\) is a union of a finite number of closed points and
\(A\) is a quotient of a regular ring. Moreover, \(E\) is locally free,
so for every \(\mathfrak{p} \in
Supp E\) one has
\(prof E_{\mathfrak{p}} = prof \mathcal{O}_{U,\mathfrak{p}}\). Finally,
if \(\mathfrak{p} \in U\) and \(c(\mathfrak{p}) = 1\), one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof E′\_𝔭 = prof E\_𝔭 = prof 𝒪\_\{U,𝔭\} = prof A\_𝔭 ⩾ 3 − 2 = 1.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We must now prove that the natural homomorphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Γ(U, E) ⟶ Γ(X̂, Ê)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is an isomorphism. Setting then \(\tilde{E} = u_{*}(E)\), one notes that
\texttt{Ẽ} is coherent and of depth \(\geqslant 2\) at every closed
point of \(X\). It follows that \(R^{i} f_{*}(\tilde{E})\) is coherent
for \(i = 0, 1\), where \(f: X \to X'\) denotes the canonical immersion
of \(X\) into \(X' = \operatorname{Spec}(A)\) (Exp. VIII). One then
applies (IX 1.5) and concludes that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Γ(U, E) ⟶ Γ(X̂, Ê)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is an isomorphism, since \(A\) is complete for the \(t\)-adic topology.

\begin{samepage}
One has a commutative diagram

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{       Γ(X, Ẽ) ────≃────→ Γ(U, E)}
\NormalTok{            ╲              ╱}
\NormalTok{             ╲            ╱}
\NormalTok{              ≃          ↓}
\NormalTok{               ╲        ╱}
\NormalTok{                ↘     ↙}
\NormalTok{                Γ(X̂, Ê)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}
\end{samepage}

whence the conclusion.

Now let \(E\) be a locally free coherent sheaf on \(\hat{X}\). If one
has proved that \(E\) is algebraizable, i.e.~is isomorphic to the formal
completion of a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module \texttt{Ẽ}, it is
easy to see that \texttt{Ẽ} is locally free in a neighborhood of \(Y\),
hence to prove \(Leff(X, Y)\). Let \(\hat{X}'\) be the formal spectrum
of \(A\) for the \(t\)-adic topology, which is identified with the
formal completion of \(X'\) along \(Y'\). Denote by \(f\) the canonical
immersion of \(X\) in \(X'\), by \(f'\) the canonical immersion of \(Y\)
in \(Y'\), and by \(\hat{f}\) the morphism deduced by passing to the
completions. In order that \(E\) be algebraizable, it suffices that
\(\hat{f}_{*}(E)\) be a coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}'}\)-Module,
since \(A\) is complete for the \(t\)-adic topology. Let
\(I = t\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\); this is an ideal of definition of
\(\hat{X}\).

For every \(n \geqslant 0\), set \(E_{n} = E/I^{n+1}E\). At every closed
point \(y \in Y\), the depth of \(E_{0}\) is \(\geqslant
2\); indeed, \(t\) is an \(A\)-regular element, so
\(prof \mathcal{O}_{Y_{0},y} = prof \mathcal{O}_{X,y} - 1 \geqslant 2\).
One concludes that \(\hat{f}_{*}(E)\) is coherent (IX 2.3). QED.

\textbf{Example} (Will allow comparison of the fundamental group of a
projective variety and a hyperplane section).

Let \(K\) be a field and let \(X\) be a proper \(K\)-prescheme. Let
\(L\) be an ample invertible \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Module. Let
\(t \in \Gamma(X, L)\) be an \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-regular element, which
means that, for every open \(U\) and every isomorphism
\(u: L|U \to \mathcal{O}_{U}\), \(u(t)\) is a non-zero-divisor in
\(\mathcal{O}_{U}\) (a condition that does not depend on \(u\)). Let
\(Y = V(t)\) be the subscheme of \(X\) of equation \(t = 0\).\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.}
  Condition (ii) is superfluous; see footnote on page 90.} Then:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  If, for every closed point \(x\) in \(X\), one has
  \(prof \mathcal{O}_{X,x} \geqslant 2\), then \(Lef(X, Y)\) holds;
\item
  if, moreover, for every closed point \(y \in Y\) one has
  \(prof \mathcal{O}_{X,y} \geqslant 3\), then \(Leff(X, Y)\) holds.
\end{enumerate}

This example will be treated in detail in Exp. XII.

Let \(S\) be a prescheme; one knows
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/ii/02-06-integral-finite-morphisms.html\#61-preschemes-integral-over-another}{EGA
II 6.1.2}) that the functor which to every finite flat covering
\(r: R \to S\) associates the \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Algebra
\(r_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{R})\) induces an equivalence between the category
of finite flat coverings of \(S\) and the category of locally free
coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{X}\)-Algebras. Let \(U\) be an open neighborhood
of \(Y\), and let \(r: R \to U\) be a finite flat covering of \(U\). Let
\(\hat{R}\) be the finite flat covering of \(\hat{X}\) deduced from it
by base change. One has \(\hat{r}_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{R}}) \simeq
r_{*}\hat{\mathcal{O}_{R}}\).

Suppose then that \(Lef(X, Y)\) holds. This implies that, for every
\(U\), the inverse image functor

\[ L(U) \longrightarrow L(\hat{X}) \]

is fully faithful. Indeed, let \(E\) and \(F\) be two locally free
coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Modules; \(\operatorname{Hom}(E, F)\) is
also coherent and locally free. By hypothesis the natural map

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Γ\_U(Hom(E, F)) ⟶ Γ\_\{X̂\}(Hom(E, F)̂)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is an isomorphism, whence the conclusion, since \texttt{Hom} commutes
with \texttt{̂} since everything is locally free. Now the \texttt{̂}
commutes with the tensor product, from which one deduces that the
functor which to every locally free coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Algebra
\(A\) associates the \(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\)-Algebra \texttt{Â} is
fully faithful. Better, if \(E\) is a locally free coherent
\(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Module, there is a bijective correspondence between
the commutative \(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\)-Algebra structures on
\texttt{Ê}.

\textbf{Proposition.}

Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian prescheme and let \(Y\) be a closed
part of \(X\). Let \(\hat{X}\) be the formal completion of \(X\) along
\(Y\). For every open \(U\) of \(X\), \(U \supset Y\), denote by
\texttt{L\_U} (resp. \texttt{P\_U}, resp. \texttt{E\_U}) the functor
which to every locally free coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Module (resp.
every finite flat covering of \(U\), resp. every étale covering of
\(U\)) associates its inverse image by \(\hat{X} \to X\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  If \(Lef(X, Y)\) holds, then for every open neighborhood \(U\) of
  \(Y\), the functors \texttt{L\_U}, \texttt{P\_U}, and \texttt{E\_U}
  are fully faithful.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  If \(Leff(X, Y)\) holds, then for every locally free coherent
  \(\mathcal{O}_{\hat{X}}\)-Module \(E\) (resp. \ldots), there exist an
  open \(U\) and a locally free coherent \(\mathcal{O}_{U}\)-Module
  \texttt{Ẽ} (resp. \ldots), such that \(L_{U}(\tilde{E})
  \simeq E\) (resp. \ldots).
\end{enumerate}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  Has been seen.
\item
  Follows from (i) and from the hypothesis, at least for \texttt{L\_U}
  and \texttt{P\_U}. Moreover, if \(R\) is an étale covering of
  \(\hat{X}\), there exist an open neighborhood \(U\) of \(Y\) in \(X\)
  and a finite flat covering \(R'\) of \(U\) such that \(\hat{R}'
  \simeq R\). From it one deduces a covering \(R''\) of \(Y\) which is
  étale by 1.1, so \(R'\) is étale in a neighborhood \(U'\) of \(Y\).
  QED.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{Corollary.}

If \(Lef(X, Y)\) holds, then in order that a finite flat covering \(R\)
of an open neighborhood \(U\) of \(Y\) be connected, it is necessary and
sufficient that \(R \times_{U} \hat{X}\) be connected. In particular, in
order that \(Y\) be connected, it is necessary and sufficient that the
open neighborhood \(U\) of \(Y\) be connected, or again that \(\hat{X}\)
be connected.

Indeed, in order that a ringed space in local rings
\((X, \mathcal{O}_{X})\) be connected, it is necessary and sufficient
that \(\Gamma(X, \mathcal{O}_{X})\) not be a direct product of two
non-zero rings. Now one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Γ(U, r\_*(𝒪\_R)) ≃ Γ(X̂, r̂\_*(𝒪\_\{R̂\}))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

by \(Lef(X, Y)\).

\textbf{Corollary.}

If \(Lef(X, Y)\) holds, then for every \(U\), the functor

\[ \hat{E}t(U) \longrightarrow \hat{E}t(Y) \]

is fully faithful. If \(Leff(X, Y)\) holds, then for every étale
covering \(R\) of \(Y\), there exist an open neighborhood \(U\) of \(Y\)
and a covering \(R'\) of \(U\) such that \(R' \times_{U} Y \simeq R\).

\textbf{Corollary.}\footnote{\emph{N.D.E.} Joined with 3.3 and the
  criteria 2.4 and 3.4, one obtains the following relative Lefschetz
  theorem. Let \(f: X \to S\) be a projective flat morphism of connected
  noetherian schemes and let \(D\) be an effective relative Cartier
  divisor in \(X\) which is relatively ample. If, for every \(s \in S\),
  the depth of \(X_{s}\) at each closed point is \(\geqslant 2\), then
  \(D\) is connected and, for every open \(U\) of \(X\) containing
  \(D\), the arrow \(i_{U}: \pi_{1}(D) \to \pi_{1}(U)\) is surjective.
  If, moreover, the depth of \(X_{s}\) along each closed point of
  \(D_{s}\) is \(\geqslant 3\), and if the local rings of \(X\) at its
  closed points are pure --- for example, complete intersections (cf.~X
  3.4) --- then \(i_{X}\) is an isomorphism. Cf. Bost J.-B., ``Lefschetz
  theorem for Arithmetic Surfaces'', \emph{Ann. Sci. Éc. Norm. Sup.} (4)
  \textbf{32} (1999), pp.~241--312, Theorems 1.1 and 2.1. In the case
  where \(X\) is simply a smooth and geometrically connected projective
  surface over a field, connectedness of \(D\) and surjectivity of
  \(\pi_{1}(D) \to \pi_{1}(U)\) (where \(U\) is an open containing
  \(D\)) always hold for \(D\) only nef of square
  \texttt{\textgreater{}\ 0} (cf.~loc. cit., Theorem 2.3 and also
  Theorem 2.4 for surfaces only normal and complete). In the case of an
  arithmetic surface (normal and quasi-projective) \(X\) over a ring of
  integers \(\mathcal{O}_{K}\), Bost, improving on results of Ihara
  (Ihara Y., ``Horizontal divisors on arithmetic surfaces associated
  with Belyĭ uniformizations'', in \emph{The Grothendieck theory of
  dessins d'enfants} (Luminy, 1993), London Math. Soc. Lect. Note
  Series, vol.~200, Cambridge Univ. Press, Cambridge, 1994, pp.~245--254
  or loc. cit., corollary 7.2), has shown that if a point \(P \in
  X(\mathcal{O}_{K})\), playing the role of the divisor \(D\) in the
  geometric situation, satisfies certain positivity conditions, then the
  arrow \(\pi_{1}(X) \to \pi_{1}(\operatorname{Spec} \mathcal{O}_{K})\)
  deduced from the projection was invertible with inverse the arrow
  \(\pi_{1}(\operatorname{Spec} \mathcal{O}_{K}) \to \pi_{1}(X)\)
  deduced from \(P\) (loc. cit., Theorem 1.2).}

If \(Lef(X, Y)\) holds and \(Y\) is connected, then every open
neighborhood \(U\) of \(Y\) is connected and the natural homomorphism
\(\pi_{1}(Y) \to \pi_{1}(U)\) is surjective. If, moreover,
\(Leff(X, Y)\) holds, the natural homomorphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{π₁(Y) ⟶ lim\_\{←, U\} π₁(U)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is an isomorphism. (N.B. One assumes that a ``base-point'' has been
chosen in \(Y\), which one also takes as base-point in \(X\), for the
definition of the fundamental groups.)

All of this follows trivially from Proposition 1.1 and Proposition 2.3.

\section{\texorpdfstring{3. Comparison of \(\pi_{1}(X)\) and
\(\pi_{1}(U)\)}{3. Comparison of \textbackslash pi\_\{1\}(X) and \textbackslash pi\_\{1\}(U)}}\label{comparison-of-pi_1x-and-pi_1u}
