\(H^{n-2}(\bar{U}, \bar{F})\), which extends (by definition of \(V\)) to
\(H^{n-2}(\bar{V}, \bar{F})\), also extends to
\(H^{n-2}(\bar{X}, \bar{F})\); but this shows that \(d(\bar{s}) = 0\),
that is, that \(d(s)\) is null at \(y\), which is absurd.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  Injectivity of (∗∗). Using the surjectivity of (∗), one obtains the
  exact sequence
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{0 → H⁰(X, ℋ\^{}\{n−1\}\_Y(F)) → H\^{}\{n−1\}(X, F) → H\^{}\{n−1\}(U, F)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

and one must show that every element
\(s \in H^{0}(X, \mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{Y}(F))\) is null. Let \(V\) be the
largest open set over which \(s = 0\). Suppose \(V \neq X\) and let
\(y\) be a maximal point of \(X - V\), \(\bar{X}\) a strict localization
of \(X\) at a geometric point \texttt{ȳ} above \(y\). By the inductive
hypothesis and by 1.8.1, one has the relation
\(prof_{\bar{Y}}(\bar{F}) \geqslant prof_{Y}(F) \geqslant n - 1\),
whence the fact that the map \(e\) in the diagram below is injective:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H⁰(X̄, ℋ\^{}\{n−1\}\_Ȳ(F̄)) = (ℋ\^{}\{n−1\}\_Y(F))\_ȳ {-}{-}e{-}{-}\textgreater{} H\^{}\{n−1\}(X̄, F̄) {-}{-}f{-}{-}\textgreater{} H\^{}\{n−1\}(V̄, F̄).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The same holds for \(f\) by virtue of the hypothesis; the left equality
follows from the retrocompactness hypothesis on \(U\). Let \(\bar{s}\)
be the image of \(s\) in \((\mathcal{H}^{n-1}_{Y}(F))_{\bar{y}}\); since
\(s\) vanishes over \(V\), one has \(f \cdot e(\bar{s}) = 0\), whence
\(\bar{s} = 0\), which contradicts the choice of \(y\) and completes the
proof.

\textbf{Remark 1.9.}

A result analogous to 1.8 is doubtless valid in the case where one
replaces the étale topos of a scheme \(X\) by a ``topos locally of
finite type'', that is, definable by a site locally of finite type (SGA
4 VI 1.1). To see this, one must use a result of P. Deligne (SGA 4
VI.9), asserting that there are ``sufficiently many fiber functors'' in
such a topos.

We are going to deduce from 1.8 important cases where one can determine
the étale depth.

\textbf{Theorem 1.10} (Cohomological semi-purity theorem)\footnote{Editors'
  note: Gabber proved since --- in 1994 --- the absolute cohomological
  purity conjecture of Grothendieck: if \(Y\) is a closed subscheme of
  absolute noetherian schemes of pure codimension \(c\) and \(n\) an
  integer invertible on \(X\), then \(\mathcal{H}^{q}_{Y}(\Lambda)\) is
  null if \(q \neq 2c\) and equals \(\Lambda_{Y}(-c)\) (Tate twist)
  otherwise, where one has set \(\Lambda = \mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\). See
  (Fujiwara K., ``A Proof of the Absolute Purity Conjecture (after
  Gabber)'', in \emph{Algebraic geometry 2000, Azumino (Hotaka)}, Adv.
  Stud. in Pure Math., vol. 36, 2002, pp.~153-183). For applications to
  the existence of the dualizing complex, see (SGA 5, Lect. Notes in
  Math., vol.~589, Springer-Verlag, 1977, p.~1672), exposé 1 and loc.
  cit., §8. This conjecture had been proved in the case
  \(n = \ell^{\nu}\) with \(\ell\) prime invertible on \(X\)
  sufficiently large by using \(K\)-theory crucially (Thomason R.W.,
  ``Absolute cohomological purity'', Bull. Soc. Math. France 112 (1984),
  no. 3, p.~397-406). \(K\)-theory enters Gabber's proof via the
  Atiyah-Hirzebruch-Thomason spectral sequence relating étale cohomology
  and \(K\)-theory, a method already used in Thomason's approach.
  Besides this result, the other fundamental argument is the
  generalization of the Lefschetz theorem cited in note (5), page 181.}.

Denote by \(X\) either a smooth scheme over a field \(k\), or a regular
excellent scheme
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/19-ch4-07-noetherian-completion.html\#78-excellent-rings}{EGA
IV 7.8.2}) of characteristic zero (N.B. if one admits resolution of
singularities in the sense of (SGA 4 XIX), it suffices to suppose, more
generally, that \(X\) is a regular excellent scheme of equal
characteristic). Let \(Y\) be a closed part of \(X\) and \(L\) the set

of prime numbers distinct from the characteristic of \(X\). Then one has

\[ prof^{L}_{Y}(X) = 2 codim(Y, X). \]

\emph{Proof.} It follows from 1.8 that one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_Y\^{}L(X) = inf\_\{y ∈ Y\} prof\_y\^{}L(X).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since on the other hand
\texttt{codim(Y,\ X)\ =\ inf\_\{y\ ∈\ Y\}\ dim\ O\_\{X,y\}}, one is
reduced to showing that

\[ prof^{L}_{y}(X) = 2 \dim O_{X,y}, \]

which follows from (SGA 4 XVI 3.7 and XIX 3.2).

\textbf{Theorem 1.11} (Homotopical purity theorem)\footnote{Editors'
  note: recently, de Jong and Oort have obtained the following purity
  statement: let \(\tilde{S}
  \to S\) be a resolution of singularities of the spectrum \(S\) of a
  normal noetherian local ring of dimension 2 and let \(U\) be the
  complement of the closed point \(s\) in \(S\). Suppose moreover that
  \(k(s)\) is algebraically closed. Then, for every prime number \(p\),
  in particular if \(S\) is of characteristic \(p\), the restriction
  morphism
  \(H^{1}_{\acute{e}}t(\tilde{S}, \mathbb{Q}_{p}) \to H^{1}_{\acute{e}}t(U, \mathbb{Q}_{p})\)
  is bijective (de Jong A.J. \& Oort F, ``Purity of the stratification
  by Newton polygons'', J. Amer. Math. Soc. 13 (2000), no. 1,
  p.~209-241, theorem 3.2). If \(k = \mathbb{C}\) and \(A\) is the
  completion of a surface singularity, this result is due to Mumford
  (see page 158, {[}5{]}).}.

If \(X\) is a locally noetherian scheme that is regular (resp. whose
local rings are complete intersections), \(Y\) a closed part of \(X\)
such that \(codim(Y, X) \geqslant 2\) (resp.
\(codim(Y, X) \geqslant 3\)), then one has

\[ prof^{hop}_{Y}(X) \geqslant 3. \]

It indeed follows from 1.8 that one has
\texttt{prof\_Y\^{}\{hop\}(X)\ =\ inf\_\{y\ ∈\ Y\}\ prof\_y\^{}\{hop\}(X)}.
Now the strictly local rings of \(X\) at the various points of \(Y\) are
regular rings of dimension \(\geqslant 2\) (resp. complete intersections
of dimension \(\geqslant 3\)). It then follows from the purity theorem X
3.4 that \(prof^{hop}_{y}(X) \geqslant 3\), which proves the theorem.

\textbf{Example 1.12.}

Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian scheme, \(Y\) a closed part of \(X\),
and \(n = 1\) or \texttt{2}. Then, if \(prof_{Y}(O_{X})
\geqslant n\) (\(prof_{Y}(O_{X})\) denoting the \(Y\)-depth in the sense
of coherent sheaves, cf.~1.6 a)), one also has
\(prof_{Y}(X) \geqslant n\); this is evident for \(n = 1\) and, for
\(n = 2\), it is none other than Hartshorne's theorem

(III 1). On the other hand, the analogous assertion is false for
\(n \geqslant 3\). Take for example an

affine space of dimension \(\geqslant 3\) over a field of characteristic
\(\neq 2\) and let \(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\) act by symmetry with
respect to the origin. Let \(X\) be the quotient and \(Y = {x}\) the
image of the origin in \(X\). Then \(O_{X,x}\) is a Cohen-Macaulay ring,
hence one has \(prof_{x}(O_{X}) \geqslant 3\); but the affine space
minus the origin is an étale covering of \(X - {x}\) that does not
extend to an étale covering of \(X\); hence one has by 1.4
\(prof_{Y}(X) =
2\).

The following theorem is the analogue of
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/18-ch4-06-flat-morphisms.html\#63-flatness-and-depth}{EGA
IV 6.3.1}):

\textbf{Theorem 1.13.}

Let \(f : X \to S\) be a morphism of schemes, \(Y\) a closed part of
\(X\), \(Z\) a closed part of \(S\) such that \(f(Y) \subset
Z\). Suppose that the local rings of \(X\) at the various points of
\(Y\) are noetherian and that the open sets \(X - Y\) and \(S - Z\) are
retrocompact in \(X\) and \(S\) respectively. Let \texttt{p,\ q,\ r} be
integers such that \(p \geqslant -r\), \(q \geqslant
0\), \(L\) a set of prime numbers and \(F\) a complex of abelian sheaves
of \(L\)-torsion on \(S\) such that the cohomology sheaves \(H^{i}(F)\)
are null for \(i < -r\). Suppose that

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\item
  The morphism \(f\) is locally \((p + q + r - 2)\)-acyclic for \(L\)
  (SGA 4 XV 1.11).
\item
  One has
\end{enumerate}

\[ prof_{Z}(F) \geqslant p. \]

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  For every point \(s\) of \(Z\), one has
\end{enumerate}

\[ prof^{L}_{Y_{s}}(X_{s}) \geqslant q. \]

Then one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_Y(f\^{}* F) ⩾ p + q.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We shall need the following lemma:

\textbf{Lemma 1.13.1.}

Let \(L\) be a set of prime numbers, \(n\) and \(r\) integers,
\(f : X \to S\) a morphism locally \(n\)-acyclic for \(L\). Let \(F\) be
a complex of abelian sheaves with cohomology sheaves of \(L\)-torsion
such that \(H^{i}(F) = 0\) for \(i < -r\), \(Z\) a closed part of \(S\)
such that \(S - Z\) is retrocompact in \(S\), and \(T = f^{-1}(Z)\).
Then the canonical morphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{f\^{}* (ℋ\^{}i\_Z(F)) → ℋ\^{}i\_T(f\^{}* F)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is bijective for \(i < n - r + 2\) and injective for \(i = n - r + 2\).

Set \(U = S - Z\) and \(V = X - T\), so that one has the cartesian
square

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{V {-}{-}g{-}{-}\textgreater{} U}
\NormalTok{|        |}
\NormalTok{k        j}
\NormalTok{|        |}
\NormalTok{v        v}
\NormalTok{X {-}{-}f{-}{-}\textgreater{} S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Consider the commutative diagram below whose rows are exact

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{→ f\^{}*(ℋ\^{}i\_Z(F))    → f\^{}*(H\^{}i(F))    → f\^{}*(H\^{}i(R j\_*(j\^{}* F)))    →}
\NormalTok{                       ≀}
\NormalTok{→ ℋ\^{}i\_T(f\^{}* F)     → H\^{}i(f\^{}* F)     → H\^{}i(R k\_*(k\^{}* f\^{}* F))     → ;}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

it follows that one is reduced to showing that the morphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{f\^{}*(H\^{}i(R j\_*(j\^{}* F))) → H\^{}i(R k\_*(k\^{}* f\^{}* F))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is bijective for \(i < n - r + 1\) and injective for \(i = n - r + 1\).
Now such a morphism comes from the following morphism between
hypercohomology spectral sequences

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{f\^{}* E\_2\^{}\{p,q\} = f\^{}* (R\^{}p j\_*(H\^{}q(j\^{}* F)))     ⟹ f\^{}*(H\^{}*(R j\_*(j\^{}* F)))}
\NormalTok{       ↓                                         ↓}
\NormalTok{E′\_2\^{}\{p,q\} = R\^{}p k\_*(H\^{}q(k\^{}* f\^{}* F))           ⟹ H\^{}*(R k\_*(k\^{}* f\^{}* F)).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since \(j\) is quasi-compact, it follows from (SGA 4 XV 1.10) that the
morphism \(f^{*}(E^{p,q}_{2}) \to E'^{p,q}_{2}\) is bijective for
\(p \leqslant n\) and injective for \(p = n + 1\); in particular it is
bijective for \(p + q \leqslant n - r\) and injective for
\(p + q = n - r + 1\). The conclusion follows immediately.

Let us return to the proof of 1.13. Let \(T = f^{-1}(Z)\). By 1.13.1 and
condition a), the canonical morphism
\(f^{*}(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{Z}(F)) \to \mathcal{H}^{i}_{T}(f^{*} F)\) is an
isomorphism for \(i \leqslant p + q\). It therefore follows from b) that
\(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{T}(f^{*} F) = 0\) for \(i < p\) and, for
\(i < p + q\), \(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{T}(f^{*} F)\) restricted to \(T\) is
the inverse image of a sheaf \(G_{i}\) on \(Z\). Let

\[ f_{T} : T \to Z \]

be the restriction of \(f\) to \(T\). It then follows from c) and from
the corollary that follows that
\(\mathcal{H}^{j}_{Y}(f^{*}_{T}(G_{i})) = 0\) for \(j < q\). One
concludes that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ℋ\^{}j\_Y(ℋ\^{}i\_T(f\^{}* F)) = 0 for i + j \textless{} p + q,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

since the inequality \(i + j < p + q\) entails either \(i < p\) and then
\(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{T}(f^{*} F) = 0\), or \(j < q\) and then
\(\mathcal{H}^{j}_{Y}(\mathcal{H}^{i}_{T}(f^{*} F)) = 0\). Given that
one has, with the notations of 1.0, \(\Gamma_{Y}
= \Gamma_{Y} \cdot \Gamma_{T}\), one has the spectral sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(1.13.2)    E\_2\^{}\{i,j\} = ℋ\^{}j\_Y(ℋ\^{}i\_T(f\^{}* F)) ⟹ ℋ\^{}\{i+j\}\_Y(f\^{}* F);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

since \(E^{i,j}_{2} = 0\) for \(i + j < p + q\), one sees that
\(\mathcal{H}^{k}_{Y}(f^{*} F) = 0\) for \(k < p + q\).

The theorem will therefore be proved if one proves the following
corollary (which is the particular case of 1.13 obtained by taking
\(Z = S\), \(r = p = 0\) and \(F\) reduced to degree \texttt{0}).

\textbf{Corollary 1.14.}

Let \(f : X \to S\) be a morphism, \(Y\) a closed part such that the
complementary open set \(X - Y\) is retrocompact in \(X\) and that the
local rings of \(X\) at the various points of \(Y\) are noetherian. Let
\(L\) be a set of prime numbers, \(q\) an integer, and \(F\) an abelian
sheaf of \(L\)-torsion on \(S\). Suppose

that \(f\) is locally \((q - 2)\)-acyclic for \(L\) and that, for every
point \(s\) of \(S\), one has \(prof^{L}_{Y_{s}}(X_{s})
\geqslant q\). Then one has \(prof_{Y}(f^{*} F) \geqslant q\).

1°) Reduction to the case where \(X\) and \(S\) are strictly local
schemes, \(f\) a local morphism, and \(Y\) reduced to a closed point of
\(X\).

By 1.8, to establish 1.14, one must show that one has for every point
\(y\) of \(Y\):

\[ prof_{y}(f^{*} F) \geqslant q. \]

Let \(s = f(y)\), \(\bar{s}\) a geometric point above \(s\), \texttt{ȳ}
a geometric point above \(y\) and over \(\bar{s}\), \(\bar{X}\) and
\(\bar{S}\) the strict localizations of \(X\) and \(S\) at \texttt{ȳ}
and \(\bar{s}\) respectively, \(\bar{f} : \bar{X} \to \bar{S}\) the
canonical morphism, and \(\bar{F}\) the inverse image of \(F\) on
\(\bar{S}\). Since one has the relation \(prof_{y}(f^{*} F) =
prof_{\bar{y}}(\bar{f}^{*} \bar{F})\), it suffices to show that the
hypotheses of 1.14 are preserved when one replaces \(f\) (resp. \(Y\),
resp. \(F\)) by \(\bar{f}\) (resp. \({\bar{y}}\), resp. \(\bar{F}\)).
The retrocompactness condition

follows from the noetherian hypothesis on \(O_{X,x}\), which implies
that \(\bar{X}\) is noetherian. By (SGA 4 XV 1.10 (i)), \(\bar{f}\) is
still locally \((q - 2)\)-acyclic for \(L\). Moreover the fiber
\((\bar{X})_{\bar{s}}\) of \(\bar{X}\) over \(\bar{s}\) is identified
with the strict localization of \(X_{s}\) at \texttt{ȳ}, hence satisfies
the relation \(prof_{\bar{y}}((\bar{X})_{\bar{s}}) \geqslant q\). Since
an analogous relation is trivially verified for the fibers of the
\(\bar{S}\)-scheme \(\bar{X}\) other than the closed fiber, this
completes the reduction.

2°) Case where \(X\) and \(S\) are strictly local, \(f\) a local
homomorphism, and \(Y\) reduced to the closed point of \(X\). Let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{g : U = X − \{y\} → S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

be the structural morphism of \(U\). One must show that the canonical
morphism

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{u\_i : H\^{}i(X, f\^{}* F) → H\^{}i(U, f\^{}* F)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is bijective for \(i \leqslant q - 2\) and injective for \(i = q - 1\).
Consider the commutative diagram

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i(X, f\^{}* F) {-}{-}u\_i{-}{-}\textgreater{} H\^{}i(U, f\^{}* F)}
\NormalTok{       ↖ v\_i           ↗ w\_i}
\NormalTok{          H\^{}i(S, F)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The morphism \(v_{i}\) is evidently bijective for every \(i\). On the
other hand \(g\) is locally \((q - 2)\)-acyclic for \(L\); moreover its
fibers are \((q - 2)\)-acyclic for \(L\), as follows from the fact that
\(prof_{y}(X_{s}) \geqslant q\) and that the fibers of \(f\) are
\((q - 2)\)-acyclic for \(L\); since \(g\) is quasi-compact (as \(X\) is
noetherian), it follows from (SGA 4 XV 1.16) that \(g\) is
\((q - 2)\)-acyclic for \(L\). Consequently \(w_{i}\), hence also
\(u_{i}\), is bijective

for \(i \leqslant q - 2\) and injective for \(i = q - 1\), which
completes the proof of 1.14.

\textbf{Corollary 1.15.}

Let \(f : X \to S\) be a morphism of schemes, \(L\) a set of prime
numbers, \(m\) and \(r\) integers, and \(F\) a complex of abelian
sheaves of \(L\)-torsion on \(S\) such that \(H^{i}(F) = 0\) for
\(i < -r\). Let \(x\) be a point of \(X\), \(s = f(x)\)

and suppose that the local ring \(O_{X,x}\) is noetherian. Then, if
\(f\) is locally \(m\)-acyclic for \(L\), one has the relation

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗)    prof\_x(f\^{}* F) ⩾ inf(prof\_s(F) + prof\_x\^{}L(X\_s), n)   where n = m − r + 2.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

In particular, if \(n \geqslant prof_{s}(F) + prof^{L}_{x}(X_{s})\), for
example if \(f\) is locally acyclic for \(L\), one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗∗)   prof\_x(f\^{}* F) ⩾ prof\_s(F) + prof\_x\^{}L(X\_s).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

If \(L\) is reduced to one element \(\ell\) and if one has
\(n \geqslant prof_{s}(F) + prof^{L}_{x}(X_{s})\), the preceding
inequality is an equality.

One reduces to the case where \(s\) and \(x\) are closed points by
taking the strict localizations of \(S\) and \(x\) at geometric points
\(\bar{s}\) above \(s\) and \(\bar{x}\) above \(x\) and \(\bar{s}\). If
one has the inequality \(n \geqslant
prof_{s}(F) + prof^{L}_{x}(X_{s})\), then (∗) is obtained from 1.13 by
taking \(p = prof_{s}(F)\) and \(q =
prof^{L}_{x}(X_{s})\) (the hypothesis that \(S - {s}\) is retrocompact
in \(S\) follows from the fact that \(X - X_{s}\) is retrocompact in
\(X\) and that \(f\) is surjective since it is \((-1)\)-acyclic (except
perhaps if the conclusion of 1.15 is empty)). If
\(n < prof_{s}(F) + prof^{L}_{x}(X_{s})\), the inequality (∗) is again
obtained from 1.13 by taking for example \(p = prof_{s}(F)\) and
\(q = n - p\). It remains to prove the last assertion. Let
\(p = prof_{s}(F)\) and \(q =
prof_{x}(X_{s})\); it follows from (1.13.2) that one has

\[ H^{p+q}_{x}(f^{*}(F)) \simeq H^{q}_{x}(f^{*}(H^{p}_{s}(F))). \]

Since \(prof_{s}(F) = p\), the sheaf \(H^{p}_{s}(F)\) is a sheaf of
\(\ell\)-torsion, constant on \(s\), non-zero. Consequently the sheaf
\(G = f^{*}(H^{p}_{s}(F))\) is a sheaf of \(\ell\)-torsion, constant on
\(X_{s}\), non-zero, hence contains a subsheaf isomorphic to
\(\mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z}\); since
\(H^{q}_{x}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z})\) is non-zero, one indeed has
\(H^{q}_{x}(G) \neq 0\).

\textbf{Corollary 1.16.}

Let \(f : X \to S\) be a regular morphism of excellent schemes (EGA IV
7.8.2) of characteristic zero, \(\ell\) a prime number, and \(F\) a
complex of sheaves of \(\ell\)-torsion on \(S\). Let \(x \in X\),
\(s = f(x)\); then one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_x(f\^{}* F) = prof\_s(F) + 2 dim(O\_\{X\_s,x\}).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Indeed \(f\) is locally acyclic (SGA 4 XIX 4.1). It then follows from
1.15 that one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_x(f\^{}* F) = prof\_s(F) + prof\_x(X\_s).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now one has by 1.10

\[ prof_{x}(X_{s}) = 2 \dim O_{X_{s},x}, \]

whence the result.

\textbf{Remark 1.17.}

It follows from 1.15 that 1.13 remains valid when one replaces b) and c)
by the conditions:

b′) For every point \(s \in f(Y)\), one has \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant p\).

c′) For every point \(x \in Y\), if \(s = f(x)\), one has
\(prof^{L}_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant q\).

In the case of a sheaf of sets or of groups, one has the following
theorem analogous to 1.13.

\textbf{Theorem 1.18.}

Let \(f : X \to S\) be a morphism of schemes, \(Y\) a closed part of
\(X\) such that \(X - Y\) is retrocompact in \(X\) and that, for every
point \(x\) of \(Y\), the local ring \(O_{X,x}\) is noetherian.

1°) Let \(F\) be a sheaf of sets on \(S\) and \(n\) an integer equal to
\texttt{1} or \texttt{2}. Suppose that \(f\) is locally \((n -
2)\)-acyclic and that, for every point \(s\) of \(f(Y)\), one has:

\[ prof_{Y_{s}}(X_{s}) + prof_{s}(F) \geqslant n. \]

Then one has:

\[ prof_{Y}(f^{*} F) \geqslant n. \]

2°) Let \(L\) be a set of prime numbers and \(F\) a sheaf of
ind-\(L\)-groups. Suppose that \(f\) is locally \texttt{1}-aspherical
for \(L\) (SGA 4 XV 1.11) and that, for every point \(s\) of \(f(Y)\),
one has:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_\{Y\_s\}\^{}\{hopL\}(X\_s) + prof\_s(F) ⩾ 3.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Then one has:

\[ prof_{Y}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 3. \]

\emph{Proof.} One reduces, as in 1.14 and 1.15, to the case where \(X\)
and \(S\) are strictly local schemes, \(f\) a local homomorphism, and
\(Y\) the closed point \(x\) of \(X\). Let \(s = f(x)\) be the closed
point of \(S\); one has the commutative diagram:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X − X\_s {-}{-}i{-}{-}\textgreater{} X − \{x\} {-}{-}j{-}{-}\textgreater{} X}
\NormalTok{        \textbackslash{}         |          /}
\NormalTok{         \textbackslash{}        g         /}
\NormalTok{          \textbackslash{}       |        / f}
\NormalTok{           \textbackslash{}      v       /}
\NormalTok{            \textbackslash{}   S − \{s\}  /}
\NormalTok{             \textbackslash{}    |k    /}
\NormalTok{              \textbackslash{}   v    /}
\NormalTok{                  S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

1°) a) Case \(n = 1\).

If \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 1\), then the morphism
\(F \to k_{*} k^{*} F\) is injective, hence the morphism \(f^{*} F \to
f^{*}(k_{*} k^{*} F)\) is also injective. On the other hand it follows
from the fact that \(f\) is locally \((-1)\)-acyclic that the morphism
\(f^{*}(k_{*} k^{*} F) \to (j \cdot i)_{*}(f^{*} F|_{X - X_{s}})\) is
injective. Finally, the composite morphism
\(f^{*} F \to (j \cdot i)_{*}(f^{*} F|_{X - X_{s}})\) is injective,
which shows that one has \(prof_{X_{s}}(f^{*}
F) \geqslant 1\), hence also \(prof_{x}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 1\).

If \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 1\), one considers the commutative
diagram

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{                          v}
\NormalTok{H⁰(X, f\^{}* F)          {-}{-}{-}{-}→    H⁰(X − \{x\}, f\^{}* F)}
\NormalTok{    | ≀                            ↑}
\NormalTok{    v                              |}
\NormalTok{H⁰(X\_s, f\^{}* F)        {-}{-}v′{-}{-}→ H⁰(X\_s − \{x\}, f\^{}* F);}
\NormalTok{(∗)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

By hypothesis, \(v'\) is injective, hence the same holds for \(v\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  Case \(n = 2\). One considers the commutative diagram
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{            u}
\NormalTok{H⁰(S, F)  {-}{-}{-}{-}→   H⁰(S − \{s\}, F)}
\NormalTok{   m ≀                  n ≀}
\NormalTok{   |                      |}
\NormalTok{   v                      v}
\NormalTok{            v                     w}
\NormalTok{H⁰(X, f\^{}* F) {-}{-}→ H⁰(X − \{x\}, f\^{}* F) {-}{-}→ H⁰(X − X\_s, f\^{}* F);}
\NormalTok{(∗∗)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

one must show that \(v\) is bijective. The morphism \(m\) is evidently
bijective, and, since \(f\) is \texttt{0}-acyclic, \(n\) is also
bijective.

If \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 2\), \(u\) is bijective. As one has seen in
a), the single hypothesis \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 1\) entails the
relation \(prof_{X_{s}}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 1\); consequently \(v\) and
\(w\) are injective; it then follows from (∗∗) that \(v\) is bijective.

If \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 2\), then \(g\) is \texttt{0}-acyclic
(since it is locally \texttt{0}-acyclic and its fibers are
\texttt{0}-acyclic). It follows that \(v \cdot m\) is bijective, hence
\(v\) is bijective.

If \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 1\) and \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 1\), then
one already knows that \(v\) and \(w\) are injective. Let \(z\) be a
maximal point of \(X_{s} - {x}\) (such a point exists by the hypothesis
\(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 1\)), \(\bar{Z}\) the strict localization of
\(X\) at a geometric point above \(z\), and \(f^{*} \bar{F}\) the
inverse image of \(f^{*}
F\) on \(\bar{Z}\). Consider the commutative diagram

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H⁰(S, F) {-}{-}{-}{-}→ H⁰(S − \{s\}, F)}
\NormalTok{   |m ≀                |n ≀}
\NormalTok{   v                    v}
\NormalTok{H⁰(X, f\^{}* F) {-}{-}v{-}{-}→ H⁰(X − \{x\}, f\^{}* F) {-}{-}w{-}{-}→ H⁰(X − X\_s, f\^{}* F)}
\NormalTok{   |m′ ≀                |r                       |}
\NormalTok{   v                    v                        v}
\NormalTok{H⁰(Z̄, f\^{}* F̄) {-}{-}{-}{-}→  H⁰(Z̄ − \{z̄\}, f\^{}* F̄)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The morphism \(m' \cdot m\) is evidently bijective, and it follows from
the fact that \(f\) is locally \texttt{0}-acyclic that \(n'
\cdot n\) is bijective; consequently \(m'\) and \(n'\) are also
bijective. Since \(w\) is injective, \(r\) is also injective, and
consequently \(v\) is bijective.

2°) Taking b) into account, one already knows that
\(prof_{x}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 2\).

If \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 3\), then
\(R^{1} k_{*}(k^{*} F) = 1\)\footnote{Editors' note: the trivial torsor
  is successively denoted \texttt{0} or \texttt{1} in what follows; we
  have left this double notation, which, on reflection, brings no
  ambiguity.}. Since \(f\) is locally \texttt{1}-aspherical,

one has
\(R^{1}(j \cdot i)_{*}(f^{*} F|_{X - X_{s}}) = f^{*}(R^{1} k_{*}(k^{*} F)) = 1\).
One therefore has \(prof_{X_{s}}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 3\) and consequently
\(prof_{x}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 3\).

If \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 3\), then \(g\) is \texttt{1}-aspherical
(since \(g\) is locally \texttt{1}-aspherical and its fibers are
\texttt{1}-aspherical). One therefore has
\(H^{1}(X - {x}, f^{*} F) = H^{1}(S, F) = 1\) and consequently
\(prof_{x}(f^{*} F)
\geqslant 3\).

If \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 2\) and \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 1\), one
uses the exact sequence (SGA 4 XII 3.2):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{1 → R¹ j\_*(i\_*(f\^{}* F|\_\{X − X\_s\})) → R¹(j · i)\_*(f\^{}* F|\_\{X − X\_s\}) → j\_*(R¹ i\_*(f\^{}* F|\_\{X − X\_s\})).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since \(f\) and \(g\) are locally \texttt{1}-aspherical, one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R¹(j · i)\_*(f\^{}* F|\_\{X − X\_s\}) ≃ f\^{}*(R¹ k\_*(k\^{}* F))}
\NormalTok{R¹ i\_*(f\^{}* F|\_\{X − X\_s\}) ≃ g\^{}*(R¹ k\_*(k\^{}* F));}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

the preceding exact sequence then writes in the form

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗∗∗)  1 → R¹ j\_*(i\_*(f\^{}* F|\_\{X − X\_s\})) → f\^{}*(R¹ k\_*(k\^{}* F)) {-}{-}a{-}{-}\textgreater{} j\_*(j\^{}*(f\^{}*(R¹ k\_*(k\^{}* F)))).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The hypothesis \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 2\) shows that the morphism
\(F \to k_{*} k^{*} F\) is bijective; applying \(g^{*}\), one finds,
taking into account the fact that \(g\) is locally \texttt{0}-acyclic,
\(f^{*} F|_{X - {x}} = i_{*}(f^{*} F|_{X -
X_{s}})\). The hypothesis \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 1\) shows that the
morphism \(a\) is injective (note that \(f^{*}(R^{1}
k_{*}(k^{*} F))\) is a sheaf equal to \texttt{1} outside \(X_{s}\) and
constant on \(X_{s}\)). It then follows from (∗∗∗) that one has
\(R^{1} j_{*}(f^{*} F|_{X - {x}}) = 1\), hence

\(prof_{x}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 3\).

If \(prof_{s}(F) \geqslant 1\) and \(prof_{x}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 2\),
one considers the sheaf of homogeneous spaces \(G\) defined by the exact
sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{1 → F → k\_* k\^{}* F → G → 1.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Applying to this exact sequence the exact functor \(g^{*}\) and using
(SGA 4 XII 3.1), one obtains the following commutative diagram whose
rows are exact:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{f\^{}*(k\_* k\^{}* F)       →     f\^{}* G                   → 1}
\NormalTok{        |                    |b}
\NormalTok{        v                    v u}
\NormalTok{j\_*(g\^{}*(k\_* k\^{}* F)) {-}{-}u{-}{-}\textgreater{}  j\_*(g\^{}* G)      → R¹ j\_*(g\^{}* F) → R¹ j\_*(g\^{}*(k\_* k\^{}* F)).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 2\), the morphism \(b\) is bijective
hence \(u\) is surjective, and one thus has a map with kernel reduced to
the neutral element:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{1 → R¹ j\_*(g\^{}* F) → R¹ j\_*(g\^{}*(k\_* k\^{}* F)) = R.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since \(g^{*}(k_{*} k^{*} F) \simeq i_{*}(f^{*} F|_{X - X_{s}})\)
(because \(g\) is locally \texttt{0}-acyclic), \(R\) is identified with
the first term of the exact sequence (∗∗∗); now one saw in the preceding
case that \(R = 1\) as soon as one has \(prof_{x}(X_{s}) \geqslant 1\),
which shows that \(prof_{x}(f^{*} F) \geqslant 3\) and completes the
proof of 1.18.

The following corollaries are generalizations of (SGA 4 XVI 3.2 and
3.3).

\textbf{Corollary 1.19.}

Let \(f : X \to S\) be a flat morphism with separable fibers of locally
noetherian schemes,

and \(Y\) a closed part of \(X\). Suppose that for every point
\(s \in f(Y)\), the fiber \(Y_{s}\) is rare\footnote{Editors' note:
  ``rare'' = ``of empty interior'', cf.~Bourbaki TG IX.52.} in \(X_{s}\)
and that one of the two following conditions is verified:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\item
  the closure of \(f(Y)\) is rare in \(S\).
\item
  \(X_{s}\) is geometrically unibranch at the points of \(Y_{s}\).
\end{enumerate}

Then one has

\[ prof_{Y}(X) \geqslant 2. \]

It indeed follows from the hypothesis on \(f\) that \(f\) is locally
\texttt{0}-acyclic (SGA 4 XV 4.1). One then applies 1.13. The hypothesis
\(Y_{s}\) rare in \(X_{s}\) (resp. \(f(Y)\) rare in \(S\)) is by 1.6 b)
equivalent to the relation \(prof_{Y_{s}}(X_{s}) \geqslant 1\) (resp.
\(prof_{f(Y)}(S) \geqslant 1\)). The hypothesis \(X_{s}\) geometrically
unibranch at each point of \(Y_{s}\) is equivalent to saying that the
strict localization of \(X_{s}\) at a geometric point of \(Y_{s}\) is
irreducible; knowing that \(Y_{s}\) is rare in \(X_{s}\), this evidently
entails \(prof_{Y_{s}}(X_{s}) \geqslant 2\), by 1.8. In either case 1.13
indeed gives \(prof_{Y}(X) \geqslant 2\).

\textbf{Corollary 1.20.}

Let \(f : X \to S\) be a regular morphism
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/18-ch4-06-flat-morphisms.html\#68-regular-normal-reduced-smooth-morphisms}{EGA
IV 6.8.1}) of locally noetherian schemes, \(Y\) a closed part of \(X\).
Suppose that, for every point \(s \in f(Y)\), one of the following
conditions is realized:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\item
  One has \(codim(Y_{s}, X_{s}) \geqslant 2\).
\item
  One has \(codim(Y_{s}, X_{s}) \geqslant 1\) and
  \(prof_{s}(S) \geqslant 1\).
\item
  One has \(prof^{hop}_{s}(S) \geqslant 3\).
\end{enumerate}

Then one has

\[ prof^{hop}_{Y}(X) \geqslant 3. \]

This indeed follows from 1.18, given that hypothesis a) implies
\(prof^{hop}_{Y_{s}}(X_{s}) \geqslant 3\) (cf.~1.11), and that the
condition \(codim(Y_{s}, X_{s}) \geqslant 1\) evidently implies
\(prof_{Y}(X) \geqslant 2\).

\section{2. Technical lemmas}\label{technical-lemmas}

\textbf{2.1.} Let \(S\) be a locally noetherian scheme, \(f : X \to S\)
a morphism locally of finite type, \(t\) a point of \(S\). If
\(x \in X\) is such that \(s = f(x) \in \operatorname{Spec} O_{S,t}\),
one sets

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ\_t(x) = deg.tr. k(x)/k(s) + dim(\{s\}),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \({s}\) denotes the closure of \(s\) in
\(\operatorname{Spec} O_{S,t}\), \(k(x)\) and \(k(s)\) the residue
fields of \(x\) and \(s\) respectively. If \(S\) is a local ring with
closed point \(t\), one writes also \(\delta(x)\) instead of
\(\delta_{t}(x)\) (cf.~SGA 4 XIV 2.2).

\textbf{Lemma 2.1.1.}

Let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X′ {-}{-}h{-}{-}\textgreater{} X}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{f′        f}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{v         v}
\NormalTok{S′ {-}{-}g{-}{-}\textgreater{} S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

be a cartesian square, where \(S\) and \(S'\) are noetherian local rings
with closed points \(t\) and \(t'\) respectively, \(g\) a faithfully
flat morphism such that \(g^{-1}(t) = t'\), \(f\) a morphism locally

of finite type. Let \(x' \in X'\), \(x = h(x')\), \(s = f(x)\),
\(s' = f'(x')\); then one has

\[ \delta(x') \leqslant \delta(x). \]

Moreover the preceding inequality is an equality if and only if one has:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{deg.tr. k(x)/k(s) = deg.tr. k(x′)/k(s′)   and   dim(\{s\}) = dim(\{s′\}).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

In particular, given \(x \in X\), one can find \(x'\) such that
\(\delta(x) = \delta(x')\).

One has indeed
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/18-ch4-06-flat-morphisms.html\#611-criteria-for-the-sets-usnf-or-ucnf-to-be-open}{EGA
IV 6.11})

\[ \dim({s}) = \dim g^{-1}({s}). \]

It follows that, for every point \(s'\) of \(g^{-1}(s)\), one has the
relation \(\dim({s'}) \leqslant \dim({s})\), and that, \(s\) being
given, one can find \(s' \in g^{-1}(s)\) such that one has the equality.
Denote then by \(Z\) the schematic closure of \(x\) in the fiber
\(X_{s}\) of \(X\) at \(s\), and let
\(Z' = Z \times_{\operatorname{Spec} k(s)} \operatorname{Spec}
k(s')\). Then \(Z'\) is equidimensional of dimension
\(deg.tr. k(x)/k(s)\); one therefore has, for every point \(x' \in
Z'_{x}\),

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{deg.tr. k(x′)/k(s′) ⩽ deg.tr. k(x)/k(s),  with equality}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

when \(x'\) is a maximal point of \(Z'_{x}\). Whence immediately the
announced conclusion.

\textbf{2.2.} Let \(f : X \to S\) be a morphism locally of finite type
and \(T\) a closed part of \(S\). Let \(x \in X\), \(s = f(x)\); we
shall set

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ\_T(x) = deg.tr. k(x)/k(s) + codim(\{s\} ∩ T, \{s\}) = inf\_\{t ∈ T ∩ \{s\}\} δ\_t(x).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\textbf{Lemma 2.2.1.}

Let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X′ {-}{-}h{-}{-}\textgreater{} X}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{f′        f}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{v         v}
\NormalTok{S′ {-}{-}g{-}{-}\textgreater{} S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

be a cartesian square, where the schemes \(S\) and \(S'\) are locally
noetherian, catenary, the morphism \(f\) locally of finite type, and
\(g\) faithfully flat. Let \(T\) be a closed part of \(S\), \(T'\) a
closed part of \(S'\) such that \(g(T')
\subset T\), \(x'\) an element of \(X'\), \(x = h(x')\) and

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{h\_\{x′\} : Spec O\_\{X′,x′\} → Spec O\_\{X,x\}}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

the morphism induced by \(h\). Then one has:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ\_T(x) − δ\_\{T′\}(x′) ⩽ dim h\_\{x′\}\^{}\{−1\}(x).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(s' = f'(x')\), \(s = f(x)\). By definition:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ\_T(x) − δ\_\{T′\}(x′) = deg.tr. k(x)/k(s) − deg.tr. k(x′)/k(s′)}
\NormalTok{                     + codim(\{s\} ∩ T, \{s\}) − codim(\{s′\} ∩ T′, \{s′\}).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since \(g\) is faithfully flat, it follows from
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/18-ch4-06-flat-morphisms.html\#61-flatness-and-dimension}{EGA
IV 6.1.4}) that one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗) codim(\{s\} ∩ T, \{s\}) = codim(g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}) ∩ g\^{}\{−1\}(T), g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}))}
\NormalTok{                       ⩽ codim(g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}) ∩ T′, g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}));}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

since \(S'\) is catenary, one has, by
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/01-ch0-14-combinatorial-dimension.html\#143-the-chain-condition}{EGA
0\_IV 14.3.2} b)):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{codim(\{s′\} ∩ T′, g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\})) = codim(\{s′\} ∩ T′, \{s′\}) + codim(\{s′\}, g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}))}
\NormalTok{   = codim(\{s′\} ∩ T′, g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}) ∩ T′) + codim(g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}) ∩ T′, g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\})).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

One deduces from this relation and (∗)

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ\_T(x) − δ\_\{T′\}(x′) ⩽ deg.tr. k(x)/k(s) − deg.tr. k(x′)/k(s′) + codim(\{s′\}, g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\})).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let us compute \(codim({s'}, g^{-1}({s})) = \dim O_{S'_{s}, s'}\) (where
\(S'_{s}\) is the fiber of \(S'\) at \(s\)). Let \(Z\) be the closed
image of \(x\) in \(X_{s}\) and \(Z' \subset X'_{s}\) the scheme defined
by the cartesian square

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Z′ {-}{-}{-}{-}→ Z}
\NormalTok{|        |}
\NormalTok{v        v}
\NormalTok{S′\_s {-}{-}→ Spec k(s)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The morphism \(Z \to \operatorname{Spec} k(s)\) is flat, locally of
finite type, and one has \(\dim Z = deg.tr. k(x)/k(s)\). It then follows
from
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/18-ch4-06-flat-morphisms.html\#61-flatness-and-dimension}{EGA
IV 6.1.2}) that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{dim(O\_\{Z′,x′\}) = dim(O\_\{S′\_s, s′\}) + deg.tr. k(x)/k(s) − deg.tr. k(x′)/k(s′);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

taking into account the fact that
\(Z'_{s'} \simeq Z \otimes_{k(s)} k(s')\), one obtains:

\[ \delta_{T}(x) - \delta_{T'}(x') \leqslant \dim(O_{Z',x'}). \]

Now \(\operatorname{Spec}(O_{Z',x'})\) is identified with the fiber at
\(x\) of the morphism

\[ (\operatorname{Spec}(O_{X',x'}))_{s} \to (\operatorname{Spec}(O_{X,x}))_{s}, \]

hence also with the fiber at \(x\) of \(h_{x'}\), which proves the
theorem.

\textbf{2.3.} The proofs of the theorems of §4 are based on duality
theory; they use the following lemmas. Let \(m\) be an integer that is a
power of a prime number \(\ell\); if \(X\) is a scheme, all the sheaves
considered on \(X\) are sheaves of \(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\)-modules;
one then has

the notion of dualizing complex on \(X\) (SGA 5 I 1.7). Suppose there
exists such a complex \(K\) on \(X\); then, for each geometric point
\(\bar{x}\) above a point \(x\) of \(X\), one deduces from \(K\)
(cf.~SGA 5 I 4.5) a dualizing complex \(K_{\bar{x}}\) on
\(\operatorname{Spec} k(\bar{x})\), so that one has
\(K_{\bar{x}} \simeq \mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}[n]\) (the bracket denoting
the translation functor) for some integer \(n\) depending only on \(x\).
We shall set

\[ \delta^{K}(x) = n. \]

If \(K\) is normalized at the point \(x\) (SGA 5 I 4.5), one therefore
has \(n = 0\).

\textbf{Lemma 2.3.1.}

Let \(X\) be a locally noetherian scheme equipped with a dualizing
complex \(K\). If \(x\) and \(x'\) are two points of \(X\) such that
\(x\) is a specialization of \(x'\) and that \(codim({x}, {x'}) = 1\),
then one has

\[ \delta^{K}(x) = \delta^{K}(x') - 2. \]

One can first reduce to the case where \(X\) is a strictly local scheme.
Indeed, let \(\bar{X}\) be the strict localization of \(X\) at a
geometric point \(\bar{x}\) above \(x\), \(i : \bar{X} \to X\) the
canonical morphism, and \(\bar{x}'\) a geometric point of \(\bar{X}\)
above \(x'\). Then \(i^{*} K\) is a dualizing complex on \(\bar{X}\) and
one has (SGA 5 I 4.5)

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(i\^{}* K)\_\{x̄\} ≃ K\_\{x̄\} and (i\^{}* K)\_\{x̄′\} ≃ K\_\{x̄′\},}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

which completes the reduction to the strictly local case.

If \(j : {x'} \to X\) denotes the immersion of the reduced closed
subscheme of \(X\) with underlying space \({x'}\), then \(R^{!} j(K)\)
is a dualizing complex on \({x'}\) and one sees immediately, using (SGA
5 I 4.5), that it suffices to prove the lemma for \({x'}\). One is thus
reduced to the case where \(X\) is a strictly local integral scheme of
dimension \texttt{1}.

Let then \(X'\) be the normalization of \(X\) and \(f : X' \to X\) the
canonical morphism; \(f\) is an integral, surjective, radicial morphism,
and it follows that \(f^{*} K\) is a dualizing complex on \(X'\) and
that it suffices to prove the lemma for \(X'\) and for the points above
\(x\) and \(x'\). One is thus reduced to the case where \(X\) is a
regular integral local scheme of dimension \texttt{1}, but one knows
(cf.~SGA 5 I 4.6.2 and 5.1) that then \(\mu_{m}[2]\) and
\(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\) are dualizing complexes, normalized
respectively at the points \(x\) and \(x'\); the lemma follows
immediately.

\textbf{Lemma 2.3.2.}

Let \(S\) be a noetherian local scheme, \(f : X \to S\) a morphism of
finite type. If \(K\) is a dualizing complex on \(S\), normalized at the
closed point \(t\) of \(S\), and if \(R^{!} f(K) = K'\) is a dualizing
complex on \(X\) (cf.~SGA 5 I 3.4.3), one has, for every point \(x\) of
\(X\):

\[ \delta^{K'}(x) = 2 \delta(x). \]

Indeed let \(s = f(x)\) and \(x'\) a closed point of the fiber
\(X_{s}\); then one has \(\delta^{K'}(x') = \delta^{K}(s)\) and by 2.3.1

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ\^{}K(s) = 2 codim(\{t\}, \{s\}) = 2 dim(\{s\}).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since one can choose for \(x'\) a specialization of \(x\), one has by
2.3.1

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ\^{}\{K′\}(x) = δ\^{}\{K′\}(x′) + 2 codim(\{x\}, \{x′\}) = δ\^{}\{K′\}(x′) + 2 deg.tr. k(x)/k(s);}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

the lemma follows immediately.

The following lemma will be used only for the converse of the Lefschetz
theorem in §4:

\textbf{Lemma 2.3.3.}

Let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X′ {-}{-}h{-}{-}\textgreater{} X}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{f′        f}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{v         v}
\NormalTok{S′ {-}{-}g{-}{-}\textgreater{} S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

be a cartesian square, where \(S\) is a strictly local excellent scheme
of characteristic zero, \(S'\) the completion of \(S\), and \(f\) a
morphism of finite type. Let \(\ell\) be a prime number, \(x \in X\),
\(Z\) the

schematic closure of \(X'_{x}\) in \(X'\), and \(i : X'_{x} \to Z\),
\(j : Z \to X\) the canonical morphisms. Then, if \(k : X'
\to R\) is a closed immersion of \(X'\) into a regular excellent scheme
\(R\) of characteristic zero, the complex

\[ K' = i^{*}(R^{!}(k \cdot j)(\mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z})) \]

is a dualizing complex on \(X'_{x}\) that is constant (that is, having
only one non-null cohomology sheaf, isomorphic to
\(\mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z}\)).

Taking (SGA 5 I 3.4.3) into account, the only thing to prove is that
\(K'\) is constant. Now, since \(Z\) is excellent, the set of points of
\(Z\) whose local rings are regular is an open set \(U\)
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/19-ch4-07-noetherian-completion.html\#78-excellent-rings}{EGA
IV 7.8.3} (iv)), and \(U\) evidently contains \(X'_{x}\) which is
regular. Let then

\[ u : U \to R \]

be the canonical immersion of \(U\) in \(R\); it follows from the purity
theorem (SGA 4 XIX 3.2 and 3.4) and from the isomorphism

\[ (\mu_{\ell})_{S} \simeq (\mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z})_{S} \]

(\(S\) strictly local) that one has

\[ R^{!} u(\mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z}) \simeq \mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z}[2c], \]

where \(c\) is a locally constant function on \(U\), necessarily
constant in a neighborhood of \(X'_{x}\), since the fibers of \(g\) are
geometrically integral by
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/31-ch4-18-complements-etale-morphisms.html\#189-formal-fibres-of-henselian-noetherian-rings}{EGA
IV 18.9.1}) hence \(X'_{x}\) integral. The lemma follows immediately.

\section{3. Converse of the affine Lefschetz
theorem}\label{converse-of-the-affine-lefschetz-theorem}

The present section will be used in §4 to prove a converse to the
``Lefschetz theorem''; a reader interested only in the direct part of
the said theorem may therefore omit the reading of the present section.

\textbf{3.1.} Let us recall the statement of the affine Lefschetz
theorem\footnote{Editors' note: Gabber has proved the following
  generalization. Let \(Y\) be a strictly local scheme of arithmetic
  type over a regular noetherian scheme \(S\) of dimension
  \(\leqslant 1\). Let \(f : X \to Y\) be an affine morphism of finite
  type, \(\Lambda = \mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}\) with \(n\) invertible on
  \(X\) and \(F\) a \(\Lambda\)-sheaf. Then \(H^{q}(X, F) = 0\) if
  \(q > \delta(F)\). From this one deduces the following local Lefschetz
  theorem. Let \(O\) be strictly local of arithmetic type over \(S\).
  For every \(f \in O\) not a zero divisor and every \(\Lambda\)-sheaf
  \(F\) on \(\operatorname{Spec}(O[f^{-1}])\), one has
  \(H^{q}(\operatorname{Spec}(O[f^{-1}]), F) = 0\) for \(q > \dim(O)\).
  Cf. (Fujiwara K., ``A Proof of the Absolute Purity Conjecture (after
  Gabber)'', in \emph{Algebraic geometry 2000, Azumino (Hotaka)}, Adv.
  Stud. in Pure Math., vol.~36, 2002, p.~153-183, §5) and especially the
  article of Illusie (Illusie L., ``Perversité et variation'',
  Manuscripta Math. 112 (2003), p.~271-295). This result is one of the
  crucial points used by Gabber in his proof of the Grothendieck purity
  theorem (cf.~note (1), page 168).} (SGA 4 XIX 6.1 bis):

Let \(S\) be a strictly local excellent scheme of characteristic zero,
\(f : X \to S\) an affine morphism of finite type, and \(F\) a torsion
sheaf on \(X\). Then, if one sets

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{δ(F) = sup\{δ(x) | x ∈ X and F\_x ≠ 0\},}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}q(X, F) = 0 for q \textgreater{} δ(F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Before stating the converse, let us prove a few lemmas.

\textbf{Lemma 3.2.}

Let \(K\) be a field, \(\ell\) a prime number distinct from the
characteristic of \(K\), and \(F\) an \(\ell\)-torsion sheaf on \(K\),
constructible, non-null. Suppose that the \(\ell\)-cohomological
dimension of \(K\) (SGA 4 X 1) is equal to \(n\) (this is realized for
example if \(K\) is the field of fractions of a strictly local excellent
integral ring of characteristic zero of dimension \(n\) (SGA 4 XIX 6.3),
or if \(K\) is a finitely generated extension of transcendence degree
\(n\) of a separably closed field (SGA 4 X 2.1)). Then one can find a
finite separable extension \(L\) of \(K\) such that:

\[ H^{n}(L, F|_{L}) \neq 0. \]

One can find a finite extension \(K'\) of \(K\) such that the
restrictions of \(F\) and \(\mu_{\ell}\) to \(\operatorname{Spec}
K'\) are constant sheaves. One then has
\(cd_{\ell}(K') = cd_{\ell}(K) = n\) (SGA 4 X 2.1), and it follows from
({[}2{]} II §3 Prop. 4 (iii)) that one can find a finite extension \(L\)
of \(K'\) such that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}n(L, μ\_ℓ) ≠ 0, i.e. H\^{}n(L, ℤ/ℓℤ) ≠ 0.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now the functor \(H^{n}(L, \cdot)\) is right exact on the category of
\(\ell\)-torsion sheaves, since \(cd_{\ell}(L) = n\); since \(F\) admits
a quotient isomorphic to \(\mathbb{Z}/\ell \mathbb{Z}\), one also has
\(H^{n}(L, F|_{L}) \neq 0\).

\textbf{Corollary 3.3.}

Let \(k\) be a field, \(K\) a finitely generated extension of
transcendence degree \(n\) of \(k\), \(F\) a constructible non-null
\(\ell\)-torsion sheaf on \(K\), with \(\ell\) prime to the
characteristic of \(k\). Then one can find a finite separable extension
\(L\) of \(K\) such that, if
\(u : \operatorname{Spec} L \to \operatorname{Spec} k\) denotes the
canonical morphism, one has

\[ R^{n} u_{*}(F|_{\operatorname{Spec} L}) \neq 0. \]

When the field \(k\) is separably closed, the corollary is a particular
case of 3.2. In the general case, one can find a finite separable
extension \(k_{1}\) of \(k\) such that the irreducible components of
\(K \otimes_{k} k_{1}\) are geometrically irreducible
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/16-ch4-04-base-field-change.html\#45-geometrically-irreducible-and-geometrically-connected-preschemes}{EGA
IV 4.5.11}); let \texttt{K\_1} be one of them. If \(k'\) is a separable
closure of \(k_{1}\), then \(K'
= K_{1} \otimes_{k_{1}} k'\) is a field, and one has by
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/16-ch4-04-base-field-change.html\#42-associated-prime-cycles-on-algebraic-preschemes}{EGA
IV 4.2})

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{deg.tr. K′/k′ = deg.tr. K/k = n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

It then follows from 3.2 that one can find a finite separable extension
\(L'\) of \(K'\) such that one has \(H^{n}(L',
F|_{L'}) \neq 0\). But \(k' = \lim_{i} k_{i}\) (direct limit), where
\(k_{i}\) runs over the finite extensions of \(k_{1}\) contained in
\(k'\), and consequently
\(K' = \lim_{i} (k_{i} \otimes_{k_{1}} K_{1})\). It follows that one

can find an index \(i\) and a finite separable extension \(L\) of
\(k_{i} \otimes_{k_{1}} K_{1} = K_{i}\) such that one has
\(L' \simeq L \otimes_{K_{i}} K'\). The extension \(L\) of \(K\) answers
the question; indeed it follows from the commutative diagram

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{        Spec L}
\NormalTok{       /     \textbackslash{}}
\NormalTok{      v       u}
\NormalTok{     /         \textbackslash{}}
\NormalTok{    v           \textbackslash{}}
\NormalTok{Spec k\_i {-}{-}w{-}{-}\textgreater{} Spec k,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

with \(w\) finite, hence \(R^{q} w_{*} = 0\) if \(q > 0\), that one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}n u\_*(F|\_\{Spec L\}) ≃ w\_*(R\^{}n v\_*(F|\_\{Spec L\})).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now \(R^{n} v_{*}(F|_{\operatorname{Spec} L}) \neq 0\), since
\(H^{n}(L', F|_{L'}) \neq 0\); one therefore also has \(R^{n}
u_{*}(F|_{\operatorname{Spec} L}) \neq 0\).

Let us recall the following well-known lemma (cf.
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iii/03-ch0-10-complements-flat-modules.html\#103-existence-of-flat-extensions-of-local-rings}{EGA
0\_III 10.3.1.2} and
\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/31-ch4-18-complements-etale-morphisms.html\#182-étale-covers}{EGA
IV 18.2.3}):

\textbf{Lemma 3.4.}

Let \(X\) be a scheme, \(x\) a point of \(X\), \(K\) a finite separable
extension of \(k(x)\). Then there exists a scheme \texttt{X\_1} étale
over \(X\), affine, and a point \(x_{1} \in X_{1}\) above \(x\) such
that \(k(x_{1})\) is \(k(x)\)-isomorphic to \(K\).

We shall use in §4 the following technical form of the converse of 3.1.

\textbf{Proposition 3.5.}

Let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{X′ {-}{-}h{-}{-}\textgreater{} X}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{f′        f}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{v         v}
\NormalTok{S′ {-}{-}g{-}{-}\textgreater{} S,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

be a cartesian square where the schemes \(S\) and \(S'\) are strictly
local excellent of characteristic zero, the morphism \(f\) locally of
finite type, \(g\) regular (EGA IV 6.8.1) surjective, with closed fiber
of \(g\) reduced to the closed point of \(S'\). Given an \(S\)-scheme
\texttt{X\_1} (resp. an \(S\)-morphism \(f_{1}\), etc.), we shall denote
by \(X'_{1}\) (resp. \(f'_{1}\), etc.) the scheme
\(X_{1} \times_{S} S'\) (resp. the morphism \((f_{1})_{(S')}\), etc.).
Let \(F\) be a constructible sheaf of \(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\)-modules
on \(X'\) (\(m\) a power of a prime number \(\ell\)) satisfying the
following conditions:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  For every point \(x \in X\), one can find a finite separable extension
  \(K\) of \(k(x)\) such that the restriction of \(F\) to the fiber
  \((X')_{(\operatorname{Spec} K)}\) comes by inverse image from a
  constructible sheaf on \(\operatorname{Spec}
  K\).
\item
  For every morphism \(f_{1} : X_{1} \to S\), with \texttt{X\_1} étale
  over \(X\), affine, for every point \(s \in S\), and for every integer
  \(q > 0\), one can find a finite separable extension \(K\) of \(k(s)\)
  such that the restriction of \(R^{q}
  f'_{1*}(F|_{X'_{1}})\) to the fiber \(S'_{(\operatorname{Spec} K)}\)
  comes by inverse image from a constructible sheaf on
  \(\operatorname{Spec} K\).
\end{enumerate}

Let \(n\) be an integer, and suppose that for every scheme \texttt{X\_1}
étale over \(X\), affine, one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i(X′\_1, F) = 0 for i \textgreater{} n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Then, if \(\bar{x}'\) is a geometric point above the point \(x' \in X'\)
such that \(F_{\bar{x}'} \neq 0\), one has

\[ \delta(x') \leqslant n. \]

Let \(Z'\) be the set of points \(x'\) of \(X'\) such that
\(F_{\bar{x}'} = 0\). Then, if \(Z = h(Z')\), one has by (i) \(Z' =
h^{-1}(Z)\); let \(x' \in X'\), \(x = h(x')\), \(s' = f'(x')\),
\(s = f(x)\). It follows from 2.1.1 and from the fact that the function
\(\delta\) decreases under specialization that it suffices to prove the
inequality \(\delta(x') \leqslant n\) when \(x\) is a maximal point of
\(Z\) and \(x'\) is such that

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{r = deg.tr. k(x)/k(s) = deg.tr. k(x′)/k(s′) and d = dim \{s\} = dim \{s′\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(x'\) be such a point; it suffices to show that one can find a
scheme \texttt{X\_1} étale over \(X\), affine, such that one has

\[ H^{d+r}(X'_{1}, F) \neq 0. \]

The set \(Z'\) is constructible (SGA 4 IX 2.4), hence the same holds for
\(Z\)
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/12-ch4-01-relative-finiteness-conditions.html\#19-pro-constructible-and-ind-constructible-sets}{EGA
IV 1.9.12}); one can then suppose, by restricting \(X\) to a
neighborhood of \(x\), that \(Z\) is an irreducible closed set with
generic point \(x\). Let \(T = f(Z)\); \(T\) is a constructible set
contained in \({s}\); one can therefore find an affine open \(U\) of
\(S\) such that \(s \in U\) and that \(T \cap U = T_{U}\) is an
irreducible closed set of \(U\) with generic point \(s\).

Let then \(V\) be a scheme étale over \(X\), affine, whose image in
\(X\) contains \(x\) and whose image in \(S\) is contained in \(U\); let
\texttt{Z\_V} be the inverse image of \(Z\) in \(V\) and
\(u : Z_{V} \to T_{U}\) the canonical morphism. Let \(W\) be a scheme
étale over \(U\), affine; we then denote by \texttt{T\_W} the inverse
image of \texttt{T\_U} in \(W\) and let \(X_{1} = W \times_{U} V\).
Since \(F\) is null outside \(Z'\), one has the spectral sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E\_2\^{}\{pq\} = H\^{}p((T\_W)′, R\^{}q u′\_*(F|\_\{(Z\_V)′\})) ⟹ H\^{}\{p+q\}(X′\_1, F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We shall show that one can choose \(V\) and \(W\) such that one has

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\alph{enumi})}
\item
  \(E^{pq}_{2} = 0\) for \(p > d\) and for \(q > r\).
\item
  \(E^{dr}_{2} \neq 0\).
\end{enumerate}

It will then follow from the spectral sequence that
\(H^{d+r}(X'_{1}, F) \neq 0\).

1°) Set \(G^{q} = R^{q} u'_{*}(F|_{(Z_{V})'})\); then one has:

\[ (G^{q})_{s'} = H^{q}((Z_{V})'_{s'}, F|_{(Z_{V})'_{s'}}), \]

since \(s'\) is a maximal point of \((T_{U})'\). Since the fiber
\((Z_{V})'_{s'}\) is an affine scheme of finite type of dimension \(r\)
over a separably closed field, it follows from 3.1 that one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(G\^{}q)\_\{s′\} = 0 for q \textgreater{} r.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

For \(q > r\), let \(Y'_{q}\) be the set of points of \((T_{U})'\) where
the geometric fiber of \(G^{q}\) is \(\neq 0\) and \(Y_{q}
= g(Y'_{q})\); then one has \(Y'_{q} = g^{-1}(Y_{q})\) by (ii), so
\(Y_{q}\) is a constructible subset of \texttt{T\_U} (SGA 4 XIX 5.1 and
EGA 1.9.12) which does not contain \(s\); restricting \(U\) to an open
neighborhood of \(s\), one can suppose that one has \(G^{q} = 0\) for
\(q > r\), hence \(E^{pq}_{2} = 0\) for \(q > r\).

Moreover, since \((T_{W})'\) is an affine scheme of finite type over
\(g^{-1}({s})\), one has, whatever \(q\) (cf.~3.1):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}p((T\_W)′, G\^{}q) = 0 for p \textgreater{} dim g\^{}\{−1\}(\{s\}) = d,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

whence condition a).

2°) Let us show that one can choose \(V\) such that
\((G^{r})_{s'} \neq 0\). By (i), there exists a constructible sheaf
\(I\), defined on a finite separable extension \(K\) of \(k(x)\), whose
inverse image on \((X')_{(\operatorname{Spec} K)}\) is isomorphic to
\(F|_{(X')_{(\operatorname{Spec} K)}}\). By 3.3, one finds a finite
separable extension \(L\) of \(K\) such that, if
\(v : \operatorname{Spec} L \to \operatorname{Spec} k(s)\) denotes the
canonical morphism, one has \(R^{r}
v_{*}(I) \neq 0\). Since the morphism
\(S'_{s} \to \operatorname{Spec} k(s)\) is regular, one has by (SGA 4
XIX 4.2):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{R\^{}r v′\_*(F|\_\{(Spec L)′\}) ≃ (R\^{}r v\_*(I))′ ≠ 0.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

By Lemma 3.4, one can find a scheme \texttt{X\_2} étale over \(X\),
affine, and a point \(x_{2}\) of \texttt{X\_2} above \(x\) such that
\(L\) is \(k(x)\)-isomorphic to \(k(x_{2})\), and one can suppose
\texttt{X\_2} over \(U\). Since \(x\) is a maximal point of \(Z\), one
has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Spec L ≃ lim ← \_V Z\_V,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(V\) runs over the affine open neighborhoods of \(x_{2}\). One
deduces by passage to the limit (SGA 4 VII 5.8), after restriction to
the geometric fiber at \(s'\):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(R\^{}r v′\_*(F|\_\{Spec L\})′)\_\{s′\} = lim → \_V (R\^{}r u′\_*(F|\_\{(Z\_V)′\}))\_\{s′\},}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

which shows that one can find \(V\) such that \((G^{r})_{s'} \neq 0\).

3°) The scheme \(V\) having been chosen in 2°), let us show that one can
choose the scheme \(W\) such that one has

\[ E^{dr}_{2} = H^{d}((T_{W})', G^{r}) \neq 0. \]

By (ii), there exists a constructible sheaf \(J\), defined on a finite
separable extension \(K\) of \(k(s)\), whose inverse image on
\((S')_{(\operatorname{Spec} K)}\) is isomorphic to
\(G^{r}|_{(S')_{(\operatorname{Spec} K)}}\). By Lemma 3.2, one can find
a finite separable extension \(L\) of \(K\) such that one has
\(H^{d}(\operatorname{Spec} L, J) \neq 0\). Since the morphism
\((S')_{(\operatorname{Spec} L)} \to \operatorname{Spec} L\) is acyclic
(SGA 4 XIX 4.1 and XV 1.10 and 1.16), one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}d((Spec L)′, G\^{}r|\_\{(S′)\_\{(Spec L)\}′\}) = H\^{}d(Spec L, J) ≠ 0.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

By 3.4, one can find a scheme \texttt{U\_1} étale over \(U\), affine,
and a point \(s_{1}\) above \(s\), such that \(k(s_{1})\) is
\(k(s)\)-isomorphic to \(L\). Now, \(s\) being a maximal point of
\texttt{T\_U}, one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Spec L ≃ lim ← \_W T\_W,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(W\) runs over the affine open neighborhoods of \(s_{1}\). One
deduces that \((\operatorname{Spec} L)' \simeq\)

\(\lim \leftarrow {}_{W} (T_{W})'\), and by passage to the limit (SGA 4
VII 5.8):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}d((Spec L)′, G\^{}r|\_\{(Spec L)′\}) ≃ lim → \_W H\^{}d((T\_W)′, G\^{}r|\_\{(T\_W)′\});}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

consequently one can find \(W\) such that one has

\[ H^{d}((T_{W})', G^{r}|_{(T_{W})'}) \neq 0, \]

which completes the proof of the theorem.

\textbf{Corollary 3.6.}

The hypotheses concerning \(S, S', f, f', m\) are those of 3.5. Denote
now by \(F\) a complex of sheaves of \(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\)-modules
on \(X'\), bounded below and with constructible cohomology, and whose
cohomology sheaves satisfy conditions (i) and (ii) of 3.5. Let \(n\) be
an integer, and suppose that, for every scheme \texttt{X\_1} étale over
\(X\), affine, one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i(X′\_1, F) = 0 for i \textgreater{} n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Then, if \(\bar{x}'\) is a geometric point above a point \(x'\) of
\(X'\) such that, for some integer \(j\),
\((H^{j}(F))_{\bar{x}'} \neq 0\), one has

\[ \delta(x') \leqslant n - j. \]

Let \(T'\) be the set of points of \(X'\) where the conclusion of 3.7
fails, and suppose \(T' \neq \emptyset\); let \(T =
f(T')\), \(x\) a maximal point of \(T\), and \(x'\) a point of \(X'\)
above

\(x\). Let \(j\) be the largest integer such that
\((H^{j}(F))_{\bar{x}'} \neq 0\); one therefore has
\(r = \delta(x) > n - j\). Let \(Z'_{q}\) be the set of points where the
geometric fiber of \(H^{q}(F)\) is \texttt{=\ 0} and
\(Z_{q} = h(Z'_{q})\); as in the proof of 3.5, \(Z_{q}\) is
constructible. One evidently has \(Z'_{q} = \emptyset\) for \(q > n\)
and for \(q\) sufficiently small. The other values of \(q\) are
distributed in three subsets. Let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Q\_1 = \{q | x ∈ Z\_q and a generization of x distinct from x is ∉ Z\_q\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

One has \(j \in Q_{1}\) and one can find an affine open neighborhood
\texttt{U\_1} of \(x\) such that, for every \(q \in Q_{1}\),
\(U_{1} \cap Z_{q}\) is an irreducible closed set with generic point
\(x\). If \(q \in Q_{1}\), one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗)    δ(H\^{}q(F)|\_\{U′\_1\}) = δ(x)    (for the definition of δ(H\^{}q(F)) cf. 3.1).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Q\_2 = \{q | no generization of x belongs to Z\_q\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Then, if \(j < q \leqslant n\), one has \(q \in Q_{2}\), and one can
find an affine open neighborhood \texttt{U\_2} of \(x\) such that, for
every \(q \in Q_{2}\), one has \(Z_{q} \cap U_{2} = \emptyset\); thus

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗∗)   H\^{}q(F)|\_\{U′\_2\} = 0 for q ∈ Q\_2.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let finally

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Q\_3 = \{q | Z\_q contains strict generizations of x\}.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Then one can find an affine open neighborhood \texttt{U\_3} of \(x\)
such that, for every \(q \in Q_{3}\), all the maximal points of
\(Z_{q} \cap U_{3}\) are generizations of \(x\). If \(q \in Q_{3}\), one
has

\[ (\ast\ast\ast) \delta(H^{q}(F)|_{U'_{3}}) \leqslant n - q. \]

For every scheme \texttt{X\_1} étale over
\(U_{1} \cap U_{2} \cap U_{3}\), affine, consider the

hypercohomology spectral sequence

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E\_2\^{}\{pq\} = H\^{}p(X′\_1, H\^{}q(F)) ⟹ H\^{}\{p+q\}(X′\_1, F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

One has \(E^{pq}_{2} = 0\) for \(q \in Q_{2}\) by (∗∗). One has
\(E^{pq}_{2} = 0\) for \(p + q \geqslant r + j\) except perhaps for
\(p = r\), \(q = j\). Indeed this is clear if \(q \in Q_{2}\); if
\(q \in Q_{1}\), one has \(p > r\) unless \(p = r\), \(q = j\), and this
follows from 3.1 taking (∗) into account; finally if \(q \in Q_{3}\),
since \(r > n - j\), one has \(p > n - q\) and the assertion follows
from 3.1 taking (∗∗∗) into account. Given that
\(H^{r+j}(X'_{1}, F) = 0\), it follows from the spectral sequence that
one has

\[ H^{r}(X'_{1}, H^{j}(F)) = 0; \]

now this entails, by 3.5, \(\delta(x) < r\), which is absurd.

\textbf{Corollary 3.7.}

Let \(S\) be a strictly local excellent scheme of characteristic zero,
\(f : X \to S\) a morphism locally of finite type, \(m\) a power of a
prime number, \(F\) a complex of sheaves of
\(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\)-modules on \(X\), bounded below with
constructible cohomology, and \(n\) an integer. Then the following
conditions are equivalent:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\tightlist
\item
  For every scheme \texttt{X\_1} étale over \(X\), affine, one has
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i(X\_1, F) = 0 for i \textgreater{} n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  For every geometric point \(\bar{x}\) above the point \(x\) of \(X\)
  and for every integer \(j\)
\end{enumerate}

such that \((H^{j}(F))_{\bar{x}} \neq 0\), one has

\[ \delta(x) \leqslant n - j. \]

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  ⇒ (ii) is the particular case of 3.6 obtained by taking \(S = S'\).
\item
  ⇒ (i) follows immediately from 3.1, using the hypercohomology spectral
  sequence
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}p(X\_1, H\^{}q(F)) ⟹ H\^{}*(X\_1, F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\section{4. Main theorem and variants}\label{main-theorem-and-variants}

\textbf{4.0.} Let \(g : X \to S\) be a separated morphism of finite
type, \(T\) a closed part of \(S\), \(Z = g^{-1}(T)\), and \(F\) a
complex of abelian sheaves on \(X\) bounded below. We call \(i\)-th
cohomology group of \(F\) with proper support, with support in \(Z\),
the group

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i\_\{Z!\}(X/S, F) = H\^{}i\_T(S, R\^{}! g(F)),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(R^{!} g\) denotes ``direct image with proper support'' (SGA 4
XVII). In the particular case where \(g\) is proper, one simply has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i\_\{Z!\}(X/S, F) = H\^{}i\_Z(X, F).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

\textbf{Proposition 4.1.}

Let \(f : U \to S\) be a morphism of finite type, \(F\) a complex of
abelian sheaves on \(U\) bounded below. Suppose one has a factorization
of \(f\):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{U {-}{-}i{-}{-}\textgreater{} X}
\NormalTok{ \textbackslash{}      /}
\NormalTok{  \textbackslash{}    /}
\NormalTok{   f  g}
\NormalTok{    \textbackslash{}/}
\NormalTok{    S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(i\) is an open immersion and \(g\) a separated morphism of
finite type, and denote by \(G\) a complex of abelian sheaves on \(X\)
bounded below that prolongs \(F\). Let \(Y\) be a closed subscheme of
\(X\) with underlying space \(X - U\), so that one has a commutative
diagram:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{Y {-}{-}j{-}{-}\textgreater{} X}
\NormalTok{ \textbackslash{}      /}
\NormalTok{  h    g}
\NormalTok{   \textbackslash{}  /}
\NormalTok{    S}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let finally \(n\) be an integer and \(T\) a closed part of \(S\). Then
the following conditions are equivalent:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  One has \(prof_{T}(R^{!} f(F)) \geqslant n\).
\item
  The canonical morphism
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ℋ\^{}i\_T(R\^{}! g(G)) → ℋ\^{}i\_T(R\^{}! h(j\^{}* G))}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is bijective for \(i < n - 1\), injective for \(i = n - 1\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{2}
\tightlist
\item
  For every scheme \(S'\) étale over \(S\), if one denotes by \(X'\)
  (resp. \(f'\), resp. etc.) the scheme \(X \times_{S} S'\) (resp. the
  morphism \(f_{(S')}\), resp. etc.), the canonical morphism
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}i\_\{g′\^{}\{−1\}(T′)!\}(X′/S′, G′) → H\^{}i\_\{h′\^{}\{−1\}(T′)!\}(Y′/S′, j′\^{}* G′)}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

is bijective for \(i < n - 1\), injective for \(i = n - 1\).

Consider in the derived category \(D^{+}(X)\) (cf.~{[}3{]}) the
distinguished triangle

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{        j\_* j\^{}* G}
\NormalTok{       ↗      ↘}
\NormalTok{   i\_! F ←{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-} G.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

In applying to this triangle the functor \(R^{!} g\), one obtains the
triangle

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{              R\^{}! h(j\^{}* G)}
\NormalTok{             ↗            ↘}
\NormalTok{(∗)    R\^{}! f(F) ←{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-}{-} R\^{}! g(G).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let us show (i) ⇔ (ii). Indeed, by Definition 1.2, (i) is equivalent to
the relation

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{ℋ\^{}i\_T(R\^{}! f(F)) = 0 for i \textless{} n;}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

now one deduces from (∗) the exact sequence of sheaves

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{→ ℋ\^{}i\_T(R\^{}! f(F)) → ℋ\^{}i\_T(R\^{}! g(G)) → ℋ\^{}i\_T(R\^{}! h(j\^{}* G)) →,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

whence the equivalence of (i) and (ii).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\tightlist
\item
  ⇔ (iii). Indeed (i) is equivalent to saying that, for every scheme
  \(S'\) étale over \(S\), one has the relation
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗∗) H\^{}i\_\{T′\}(S′, R\^{}! f′(F′)) = 0 for i \textless{} n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Now one deduces from (∗) the exact sequence of abelian groups

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{→ H\^{}i\_\{T′\}(S′, R\^{}! f′(F′)) → H\^{}i\_\{T′\}(S′, R\^{}! g′(G′)) → H\^{}i\_\{T′\}(S′, R\^{}! h′(j′\^{}* G′)) →;}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

taking 4.0 into account, this exact sequence writes in the form

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{→ H\^{}i\_\{T′\}(S′, R\^{}! f′(F′)) → H\^{}i\_\{g′\^{}\{−1\}(T′)!\}(X′/S′, G′) → H\^{}i\_\{h′\^{}\{−1\}(T′)!\}(Y′/S′, j′\^{}* G′) →.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

The equivalence of (i) and (iii) follows, taking the form (∗∗) of (i)
into account.

\textbf{4.2.0.} When \(f : U \to S\) is affine, we shall give local
conditions on \(F\) for conditions (i) to (iii) of 4.1 to be verified.
In what follows, the schemes considered are excellent schemes of
characteristic zero, the sheaves are sheaves of
\(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\)-modules, where \(m\) is a power of a prime
number. If one had resolution of singularities in the sense of (SGA 4
XIX), the results stated, as well as their proofs, would still be valid
for excellent schemes of equal characteristic, with \(m\) prime to the
characteristic.

\textbf{Theorem 4.2.}

Let \(S\) be an excellent scheme of characteristic zero and
\(f : U \to S\) a separated morphism of finite type. Let \(F\) be a
complex of sheaves of \(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\)-modules on \(U\),
bounded below with constructible cohomology, \(n\) an integer, and \(T\)
a closed part of \(S\). Then the following conditions are equivalent:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\tightlist
\item
  For every scheme \texttt{U\_1} étale over \(U\), affine over \(S\),
  one has, denoting by \(f_{1}\) the structural morphism of
  \texttt{U\_1} and by \texttt{F\_1} the restriction of \(F\) to
  \texttt{U\_1}:
\end{enumerate}

\[ prof_{T}(R^{!} f_{1}(F_{1})) \geqslant n \]

(cf.~Prop. 4.1 on the meaning of this relation).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  For every point \(u\) of \(U\), one has:
\end{enumerate}

\[ prof_{u}(F) \geqslant n - \delta_{T}(u), \]

where one sets (cf.~2.2):
\(\delta_{T}(u) = deg.tr.(k(x)/k(s)) + codim({s} \cap T, {s})\).

\emph{Proof.}

1°) Let \(t\) be a point of \(T\), \(\bar{S}\) the strict localization
of \(S\) at a geometric point above \(t\), and \(S'\) the completion of
\(\bar{S}\) with closed point \(t'\); then \(\bar{S}\) is excellent by
(\href{https://jcreinhold.github.io/ega/iv/19-ch4-07-noetherian-completion.html\#79-excellent-rings-and-resolution-of-singularities}{EGA
IV 7.9.5}), so \(S'\) is a complete strictly local excellent scheme.
Given a scheme \(U\) over \(X\) (resp. an \(S\)-morphism \(f\), resp.
etc.), we shall denote by \(U'\) (resp. \(f'\), resp. etc.) the scheme
\(U \times_{S} S'\) (resp. the morphism \(f_{(S')}\), resp. etc.). One
has the cartesian square

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{U′ {-}{-}h{-}{-}\textgreater{} U}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{f′        f}
\NormalTok{|         |}
\NormalTok{v         v}
\NormalTok{S′ {-}{-}g{-}{-}\textgreater{} S,}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

in which the morphism \(g\) is regular (EGA IV 7.8.2). Let us show that
it suffices to prove that (for every point \(t \in
T\)) the two following properties are equivalent:

(i)\_t For every scheme \texttt{U\_1} étale over \(U\), affine over
\(S\), setting \(f_{1} : U_{1} \to S\), one has

\[ prof_{t'}(R^{!} f'_{1}(F'_{1})) \geqslant n. \]

(ii)\_t For every point \(u'\) of \(U'\), one has

\[ prof_{u'}(F') \geqslant n - \delta_{t'}(u'). \]

It suffices to prove the following lemma:

\textbf{Lemma 4.2.1.}

One has (i) ⇔ (i)\_t for every \(t \in T\) and (ii) ⇔ (ii)\_t for every
\(t \in T\).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\tightlist
\item
  ⇔ (i)\_t for every \(t \in T\). Indeed (i) is equivalent to saying
  that, for every scheme \texttt{U\_1} étale over \(U\), affine over
  \(S\), one has
\end{enumerate}

\[ prof_{T}(R^{!} f_{1}(F_{1})) \geqslant n; \]

now by 1.8

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_T(R\^{}! f\_1(F\_1)) = inf\_\{t ∈ T\} prof\_t(R\^{}! f\_1(F\_1)).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Since \(g^{*}(R^{!} f_{1}(F_{1})) \simeq R^{!} f'_{1}(F'_{1})\) (SGA 4
XVII), one has by 1.16

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_t(R\^{}! f\_1(F\_1)) = prof\_\{t′\}(R\^{}! f′\_1(F′\_1)),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

so (i) is equivalent to saying that one has, for every \(t \in T\),
\(prof_{t'}(R^{!} f'_{1}(F'_{1})) \geqslant n\), which is none other
than (i)\_t.

(ii)\_t for every \(t \in T\) ⇒ (ii). Indeed let \(u \in U\); one must
show the relation

\[ prof_{u}(F) \geqslant n - \delta_{T}(u), \]

where \(\delta_{T}(u) = \inf_{t \in T \cap {s}} \delta_{t}(u)\)
(cf.~2.2); one is therefore reduced to showing that one has, for every
\(t \in T \cap {s}\)

\[ prof_{u}(F) \geqslant n - \delta_{t}(u). \]

Let \(u'\) be a point of \(U'\) such that \(h(u') = u\) and
\(\delta_{t'}(u') = \delta_{t}(u)\) (cf.~2.1.1). Since \(h\) is locally
acyclic (SGA 4 XIX 4.1), it follows from 1.16 and from the fact that
\(u'\) is a generic point of \(U'_{u}\) that one has

\[ prof_{u'}(F') = prof_{u}(F). \]

But one has by (ii)\_t
\(prof_{u}(F) = prof_{u'}(F') \geqslant n - \delta_{t}(u)\), which
proves (ii).

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\setcounter{enumi}{1}
\tightlist
\item
  ⇒ (ii)\_t for every \(t\). With the notations of 2.2.1, for every
  point \(u'\) of \(U'\), one has by 1.16
\end{enumerate}

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_\{u′\}(F′) ⩾ prof\_u(F) + 2 dim h\_\{u′\}\^{}\{−1\}(u) ⩾ prof\_u(F) + dim h\_\{u′\}\^{}\{−1\}(u).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Taking 2.2.1 and (ii) into account, one obtains

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{prof\_\{u′\}(F′) ⩾ n − δ\_T(u) + dim h\_\{u′\}\^{}\{−1\}(u) ⩾ n − δ\_\{t′\}(u′),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

which is none other than (ii)\_t.

2°) (ii)\_t ⇔ (i)\_t. One immediately reduces to the case where \(F\) is
bounded, by truncating \(F\) at a sufficiently high rank. One can
realize \(S'\) as a closed subset of a complete regular local scheme,
hence excellent; it then follows from (SGA 5 I 3.4.3) that there exists
a dualizing complex \(K\) on \(S'\) and that \(R^{!} f'(K) = K'\) is a
dualizing complex on \(U'\). We shall choose \(K\) such that
\(\delta^{K}(t') = 0\) (for the definition of \(\delta^{K}(t')\),
cf.~2.3), and denote by \(DF'\) the dual of \(F'\) with respect to
\(K'\). One can reformulate hypothesis (ii)\_t as follows:

\textbf{Lemma 4.2.2.}

Let \(u'\) be a point of \(U'\); then the following conditions are
equivalent:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{(\roman{enumi})}
\item
  One has \(prof_{u'}(F') \geqslant n - \delta_{t'}(u')\).
\item
  One has \((H^{q}(DF'))_{\bar{u}'} = 0\) for
  \(q > -n - \delta_{t'}(u')\) (\(\bar{u}'\) geometric point above
  \(u'\)).
\end{enumerate}

Let \(\bar{U}'\) be the strict localization of \(U'\) at \(\bar{u}'\)
and \(\bar{F}'\) the inverse image of \(F'\) by the morphism
\(\bar{U}' \to U'\). The relation
\(prof_{u'}(F') \geqslant n - \delta_{t'}(u')\) is equivalent by
definition to:

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗) H\^{}i\_\{ū′\}(F̄′) = 0 for i \textgreater{} n − δ\_\{t′\}(u′).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Let \(DH^{i}_{\bar{u}'}(\bar{F}')\) be the dual of the abelian group
\(H^{i}_{\bar{u}'}(\bar{F}')\) with respect to
\(\mathbb{Z}/m\mathbb{Z}\). By 2.3.2, \(K'[-2 \delta_{t'}(u')] = K''\)
satisfies \(\delta^{K''}(u') = 0\); since \(F'\) has constructible
cohomology, one has \(DF' = D(\bar{F}')\) and the local duality theorem
(SGA 5 I 4.5.3) shows then

that one has

\[ DH^{i}_{\bar{u}'}(\bar{F}') \simeq H^{-i - 2 \delta_{t'}(u')}(DF')_{\bar{u}'}. \]

So (∗) is equivalent to the relation

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗∗) (H\^{}q(DF′))\_\{ū′\} = 0 for q \textgreater{} −n − δ\_\{t′\}(u′).}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

We are now in a position to prove the theorem. The relation (ii)\_t is
equivalent to the relation (∗∗). Let \(G^{q} =
H^{q}(DF')\); the affine Lefschetz theorem (3.1) entails in particular
that, for every scheme \texttt{U\_1} étale over \(U\), affine over
\(S\), one has

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}p(U′\_1, G\^{}q) = 0 for p \textgreater{} δ(G\^{}q),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

where \(\delta(G^{q})\) is the supremum of the \(\delta_{t'}(u')\) for
the \(u'\) such that \((G^{q})_{\bar{u}'} \neq 0\); by (∗∗) one has
\(\delta(G^{q}) \leqslant -n - q\), so (ii)\_t entails the relation

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{H\^{}p(U′\_1, H\^{}q(DF′)) = 0 for p \textgreater{} −q − n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Taking the hypercohomology spectral sequence of the functor ``sections
over \(U'_{1}\)'' with respect to the complex \(DF'\):

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{E\_2\^{}\{pq\} = H\^{}p(U′\_1, H\^{}q(DF′)) ⟹ H\^{}\{p+q\}(U′\_1, DF′),}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

one obtains the relation

\begin{Shaded}
\begin{Highlighting}[]
\NormalTok{(∗∗∗) H\^{}i(U′\_1, DF′) = 0 for i \textgreater{} −n.}
\end{Highlighting}
\end{Shaded}

Conversely, suppose the preceding relation verified, for every
\texttt{U\_1} étale over \(U\), affine over \(S\). Apply Proposition 3.6
by replacing \(S\) by \(\bar{S}\); the hypotheses of 3.6 concerning
\(S\) are satisfied, since for every scheme \texttt{U\_1} étale over
\(U\), affine, one can find a scheme over \texttt{U\_1} that comes by
inverse image from an étale scheme over \(U\) affine over \(S\); as for
the hypotheses concerning \(F\), they are satisfied thanks to 2.3.3. One
thus has, for every point \(u'\) of \(U'\) such that
\((H^{q}(DF'))_{\bar{u}'} \neq 0\):

\[ \delta_{t'}(u') \leqslant -n - q, \]
